04. Applied Science System Design: Agentic Automation, Computer-Use Agents, Document Understanding, Communications Mining, Process Mining and Orchestration

How to read this chapter. This is the ML system design chapter for UiPath's own product surface. Each section opens with a plain-text block diagram of the system you are likely to be asked to design. The numbered questions follow, with follow-up probes as indented sub-bullets. The chapter has questions only, with no answers. The diagrams are plain text so they render the same in Markdown and in a PDF export.

Tagging system. Every question carries one of two tags:

  • [Reported] [n]: a candidate report or interview question bank links this question to UiPath. The number points to the References list.
  • [Likely]: an inference from the role, the level and UiPath's products: the agentic automation platform (agents, robots, humans), computer-use agents, Document Understanding and IXP, Communications Mining, Process Mining, Autopilot, Agent Builder and coded agents, Maestro orchestration and the AI Trust Layer. No candidate has reported it. A [Likely] [n] tag means the premise of the question (a product feature, an SDK behaviour, a benchmark rule) is confirmed by source [n]. It does not mean a candidate was asked it.
  • Indented sub-bullets are follow-up probes an interviewer may use to push a strong first answer toward the principal bar.

Research limits (please read). This review pass (October 2026) could not reach any candidate-report site. The shared web-search budget for the workflow was used up. The egress proxy refused connections to glassdoor.com, uipath.com, docs.uipath.com and arxiv.org (HTTP 403 by policy). GitHub raw content was reachable, so the review read UiPath's own public SDK repositories (documentation, samples and source) and the OSWorld repository. Those sources confirm product behaviour, not interview content. So no question below is tagged [Reported], and this chapter does not invent reports. Product names such as Maestro, Agent Builder, Autopilot and IXP should still be checked against current UiPath documentation before the interview.

Format notes for this round

Item Expected shape (inferred, not confirmed)
Round name ML / applied science system design, sometimes merged with a product or "architecture" round
Duration 60 min: about 5 min framing, 35-40 min design, 10-15 min deep-dive probes, 5 min your questions
Interviewer Principal or distinguished scientist, or an AI platform architect; sometimes paired with a product lead
Prompt style Open-ended and UiPath-shaped: "design an agent that processes invoices end to end", "design a computer-use agent for legacy desktop apps", "design the eval system for agents"
Principal-level bar You set the problem framing and success metrics yourself. You separate deterministic (robot / coded function) work from probabilistic (agent / LLM) work. You design evaluation and the human-in-the-loop path as first-class parts. You reason about cost, latency, on-prem and regulated deployment, and governance. You define a multi-quarter roadmap and say what research is still open.
Common failure modes Jumping to "use GPT plus RAG" without workload analysis; no offline/online eval plan; ignoring exception handling, retries and human escalation; ignoring the existing RPA asset base (selectors, workflows, queues)

Reference diagram: the agentic automation stack an interviewer has in mind

                         ┌───────────────────────────────────────────┐

  (email, doc, ticket,   │  process model, state, SLAs, routing      │     discovered process
   API call, schedule)   └───────┬───────────────┬───────────────┬───┘     + KPIs + conformance
                                 │               │               │
                    ┌────────────▼───┐   ┌───────▼───────┐  ┌────▼────────────┐
                    │  AGENTS        │   │  ROBOTS       │  │  HUMANS         │
                    │  LLM reasoning │   │  deterministic│  │  Action Center/ │
                    │  tool calls,   │   │  RPA, APIs,   │  │  approvals,     │
                    │  context/RAG   │   │  coded funcs  │  │  exceptions     │
                    └───┬────────┬───┘   └───────┬───────┘  └────┬────────────┘
                        │        │               │               │
              ┌─────────▼──┐ ┌───▼──────────┐    │      feedback / labels
              │ Specialised│ │ Computer-use │    │               │
              │ models: DU,│ │ agent (screen│    │               ▼
              │ IXP, Comms │ │ understanding│    │      ┌─────────────────┐
              │ classifiers│ │ + actions)   │    │      │ Eval + learning │
              └────────────┘ └──────────────┘    │      │ loop (traces,   │
                                                 │      │ datasets, judges)│
   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────▼──────┴─────────────────┴──┐
   │ AI TRUST LAYER: LLM gateway, guardrails, PII masking, audit logs, quotas │
   └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

A. Agentic automation: agents + robots + humans

   Task ──> Router ──┬──> deterministic? ──> Robot (RPA workflow / coded function / API)
                     ├──> needs judgement? ──> Agent (plan, tools, memory)
                     │                           │  confidence < τ or policy hit
                     │                           ▼
                     └──> high risk / ambiguous ──> Human task (approve / correct)
                                                     │
                                       corrections ──┴──> eval set + fine-tune / prompt update
  1. [Likely] Design an end-to-end agentic system that resolves inbound customer-service emails for an insurer, using agents, existing RPA robots and human reviewers.
    • How do you decide which steps stay deterministic robots and which become agent steps?
    • What is the unit of work, and how do you define "resolved" as a metric?
    • Where exactly does a human get pulled in, and what context do they see?
  2. [Likely] [5] UiPath's SDK separates "coded functions" (deterministic, typed input/output, no LLM) from "coded agents" (an LLM reasoning loop that chooses tools). Give a decision procedure for which one a new automation should be, and design the path for promoting a function to an agent, or demoting an agent back to a function, once you have production traces.
    • What evidence from traces would justify the demotion?
    • How do you keep the typed input/output contract stable for the callers when the implementation changes?
  3. [Likely] Given a company with 500 existing RPA workflows, how would you introduce agents without breaking what works?
    • Would agents call robots as tools, or robots call agents? What changes in failure handling?
    • How do you prioritise which workflows to "agentify" first?
  4. [Likely] [10] [11] Design the confidence and escalation policy for an agent that takes financial actions (refunds, payments) inside an enterprise process.
    • How do you calibrate an LLM agent's confidence when there are no token-level probabilities you can trust?
    • How do you set thresholds per action type and per customer risk appetite?
    • How do you stop humans from rubber-stamping escalations?
  5. [Likely] [11] UiPath publishes an anti-money-laundering triage sample in which the LLM only extracts facts and writes the rationale, while a separate typed classifier makes every decision (red flags, risk level, disposition). When is this "LLM reads and writes, a calibrated model decides" split better than letting the agent decide?
    • How do you calibrate and monitor the decision model, and what happens when the LLM's extraction is wrong upstream?
    • How would you explain one disposition to a regulator?
  6. [Likely] [3] [4] How would you design the tool interface layer so that an agent can safely invoke UiPath robots, APIs, Integration Service connectors and MCP servers?
    • How do you describe tools so the model picks the right one among hundreds?
    • How do you handle tool schemas that change under the agent?
    • How do you enforce least privilege per agent and per run?
  7. [Likely] [4] UiPath lets customers host their own MCP servers on the platform and expose them to agents. Design the governance for third-party MCP tools: registration, trust review, versioning, sandboxing and per-tenant allow lists.
    • How do you detect that an MCP tool's behaviour changed even though its schema did not?
    • What do you do when a tool's description itself contains an injected instruction?
  8. [Likely] Design memory for a long-running enterprise agent that handles a case over days or weeks.
    • What goes into short-term context, case state and long-term memory?
    • How do you prevent stale or poisoned memory from driving actions?
  9. [Likely] [8] [2] Design durable suspend and resume for agents that wait days for a human approval, a robot job or an external callback. UiPath's runtime checkpoints agent state at an interrupt() and resumes it later.
    • What exactly do you checkpoint, and where, so a resume survives a container restart?
    • The agent's code is redeployed while 3,000 runs are suspended. How do you migrate or pin their checkpoints?
    • How do you guarantee a side effect performed just before the suspend is not repeated on resume?
  10. [Likely] [9] Design an event-triggered, long-running agent that sleeps on a connector event (for example, a new email in a watched mailbox), wakes to process it, then suspends again.
    • How do you handle duplicate events, out-of-order events and a burst of 10,000 events after an outage?
    • How do you stop one poison message from blocking the agent forever?
  11. [Likely] [13] How would you design a multi-agent system for procure-to-pay, and when is multi-agent (for example, a supervisor routing to specialist agents) worse than a single agent?
    • How do agents hand off context, and who owns the global state?
    • How do you debug a failure that spans three agents and two robots?
  12. [Likely] What is your framework for deciding the autonomy level of an agent in a given process?
    • What evidence moves a process from "human approves every action" to "human reviews a sample"?
  13. [Likely] [6] How would you make an agentic workflow idempotent and recoverable when a step fails halfway, for example after a payment API call times out?
    • How do you design compensation or rollback for actions an agent already took?
  14. [Likely] [7] [12] Design the human-in-the-loop task experience so that corrections become training or evaluation data.
    • What do you log from a human correction, and how do you de-bias it?
    • How do you use corrections without overfitting to one reviewer's preferences?
  15. [Likely] [12] Design the human task inbox at scale: 50,000 open approval tasks a day from agents across many processes, with different SLAs and reviewer skills.
    • How do you prioritise and assign tasks, and when do you batch similar approvals?
    • How do you measure reviewer fatigue and the quality of approvals, not just throughput?
  16. [Likely] How would you measure ROI for an agentic automation, compared with the RPA baseline and the fully manual baseline?
    • Which metrics would you show a CFO, and which would you show the science team?
  17. [Likely] [15] How would you defend an agent against prompt injection that arrives through emails, documents or web pages it processes?
    • How do you separate instructions from data in the agent's context?
    • What runtime guardrails would you add beyond the model itself?
  18. [Likely] [3] Design the system that lets an enterprise run the same agent across multiple LLM providers, or on-prem models, with consistent behaviour.
    • How do you detect and manage regression when a provider updates a model?
  19. [Likely] [2] [3] [5] UiPath's runtime hosts agents written in LangGraph, LlamaIndex, the OpenAI Agents SDK, Google ADK, PydanticAI, the Claude Agent SDK and its own framework behind one runtime protocol. What must that protocol standardise (inputs/outputs, tracing, errors, human-in-the-loop, resume) and what should it deliberately leave to the framework?
    • How do you evaluate and compare two agents built on different frameworks fairly?
    • What breaks first when a framework releases a breaking change?

B. Computer-use agents (ScreenAgent-style UI automation)

  Goal ("file this expense in SAP GUI")
     │
     ▼
  ┌──────────────┐   screenshot + UI tree / accessibility / selectors
  │ Perception   │ <─────────────────────────────────────────── Desktop / Browser / Citrix
  │ (VLM, OCR,   │                                                     ▲
  │  grounding)  │                                                     │ click/type/scroll
  └──────┬───────┘                                                     │
         ▼                                                             │
  ┌──────────────┐   plan / reflect    ┌──────────────┐   verify   ┌────┴─────────┐
  │ Planner LLM  │ ──────────────────> │ Action model │ ─────────> │ Executor     │
  │ (task graph) │ <── state diff ──── │ (grounded    │            │ (robot runtime│
  └──────────────┘                     │  coordinates)│            │ + safety gate)│
                                       └──────────────┘            └──────────────┘
         └──> trajectory log ──> replay / eval (OSWorld-like suites + customer apps)
  1. [Likely] Design a computer-use agent that can operate legacy desktop applications (for example SAP GUI or a Citrix-virtualised app) where no API or DOM is available.
    • What inputs do you use: pixels only, accessibility tree, UiPath selectors, OCR? How do you fuse them?
    • How do you ground a natural-language target ("the Submit button") to coordinates reliably?
  2. [Likely] How would you combine a generalist computer-use model with UiPath's existing deterministic selectors and recorded workflows?
    • When should the agent "compile" a successful trajectory into a deterministic robot workflow?
    • How would you detect that a compiled workflow has drifted and needs the agent again?
    • If an agent proposes a repaired selector after a UI change, how do you verify the repair before it is committed, so the robot does not silently click the wrong element?
  3. [Likely] [21] [22] How would you evaluate a computer-use agent, and how far would you trust public benchmarks such as OSWorld for enterprise use?
    • How do you build an internal benchmark of enterprise apps with verifiable end states?
    • How do you score partial progress versus binary task success?
    • How do you prevent benchmark contamination and overfitting to the leaderboard?
  4. [Likely] [21] OSWorld had to be re-released as OSWorld-Verified to fix community-reported task errors, and its maintainers now run verified submissions themselves. Design the governance for an internal computer-use benchmark so you do not repeat that history.
    • How do you detect flaky tasks and environment noise, and separate them from real capability changes?
    • How many reruns per task do you need before you report a score difference, and how do you report variance?
  5. [Likely] [22] OSWorld grades each task with an execution-based script that inspects the final machine state. Design state verifiers for enterprise tasks where the true end state lives in a backend you cannot read directly (SAP posting, Salesforce record, mainframe screen).
    • When is a screen-based check acceptable, and when must you query the system of record?
    • How do you keep verifiers correct as the application is upgraded?
  6. [Likely] Design the safety layer for a computer-use agent that has a real user's desktop session.
    • Which actions require confirmation, and how does the agent know an action is irreversible?
    • How do you handle unexpected dialogs, pop-ups and login prompts?
  7. [Likely] Latency is 5-10 seconds per step and tasks take 40 steps. How do you make this usable and affordable?
    • Where would you use a smaller grounding model versus a frontier planner?
    • Which steps can be cached or replayed without calling a model?
  8. [Likely] Build the break-even model: for a given process, at what volume, UI change rate and error cost is a computer-use agent cheaper than a maintained RPA robot, and when is it cheaper than a human?
    • Which inputs to this model would you measure first, and from which telemetry?
  9. [Likely] How would you collect training data for UI grounding and action prediction at scale?
    • How would you use existing robot executions and selector data as weak supervision?
    • What are the privacy constraints of using customer screenshots, and how do you design around them?
  10. [Likely] How does the agent recover when it gets lost (wrong screen, loop, state it did not expect)?
    • How do you detect loops and lack of progress automatically?
    • When should it give up and hand off to a human, and with what summary?
  11. [Likely] How would you handle high-DPI, multi-monitor, localisation (non-English UIs) and theme variation in perception?
  12. [Likely] Compare a pixel-only VLM agent with a structured-UI (accessibility tree / DOM) agent for enterprise automation. Where does each break?
  13. [Likely] How would you fine-tune or distil a computer-use model for a single customer's application portfolio, and how do you keep that maintainable?

C. Document Understanding and IXP pipelines

  Ingest (scan, PDF, email attachment)
     │
     ▼
  Digitise: OCR / layout ──> Classify (doc type, split multi-doc bundles)
     │                                 │
     ▼                                 ▼
  Extract fields & tables  <── model choice: specialised ML extractor | generative LLM | hybrid
     │
     ▼
  Validate: business rules, cross-doc checks, confidence per field
     │                    │
     │ high confidence    │ low confidence / rule fail
     ▼                    ▼
  Downstream robot     Validation action (human) ──> labelled data ──> retrain / prompt eval
  1. [Likely] Design an invoice-processing pipeline that handles 1M documents a month across 40 languages and thousands of vendor layouts.
    • Where do you use a trained extractor and where a generative model?
    • How do you hit a straight-through-processing target while keeping field-level accuracy above a contract threshold?
  2. [Likely] [20] UiPath's SDK exposes classification, extraction (DU Modern and IXP projects) and human validation actions as separate, non-blocking start/retrieve operations. Design the asynchronous pipeline around them for 1M documents a month.
    • How do you track a document across classify, extract, validate and downstream steps when any of them can fail or time out?
    • What decides that a document goes to a validation action, and at which granularity (document, page, field)?
  3. [Likely] Suppose one extraction product must serve both structured documents and unstructured inputs such as emails and long contracts. What shared representation, schema language and confidence model would you design, and what gets worse when you merge the two workloads?
  4. [Likely] How would you produce field-level confidence scores that are actually calibrated, for both classic extractors and LLM-based extraction?
    • How do you set per-field thresholds that trade human review cost against error cost?
    • How do you show calibration to a customer?
  5. [Likely] Design document classification and splitting for a 300-page mortgage packet containing 25 document types.
    • How do you handle unseen document types at run time?
  6. [Likely] Design generative extraction from 200-page commercial contracts where the answer to one field (for example, the termination notice period) depends on definitions, amendments and cross-references spread through the document.
    • How do you chunk, retrieve and resolve conflicting clauses?
    • How do you evaluate this when ground truth needs a lawyer's time?
  7. [Likely] How would you extract complex tables (line items spanning pages, merged cells, nested headers) robustly?
    • How do you evaluate table extraction: cell-level, row-level, or downstream reconciliation?
  8. [Likely] A customer has 50 labelled examples of a new document type. Design the fastest route to production quality.
    • When is few-shot prompting enough, and when do you fine-tune?
    • How do you use active learning to pick the next documents to label?
  9. [Likely] [20] How would you design the human validation loop so that corrections improve the model without introducing label noise?
    • How do you detect inconsistent annotators?
  10. [Likely] Handwriting, low-quality scans and stamps degrade OCR. Where in the pipeline do you invest, and how do you measure the gain end to end?
  11. [Likely] How would you ground every extracted value back to a location in the document, and why does that matter for audit?
    • How do you stop a generative extractor from producing a plausible value that is not in the document?
  12. [Likely] Design the evaluation harness for Document Understanding that runs on every model or prompt change.
    • What is in the golden set, how is it stratified, and how do you keep it from going stale?
  13. [Likely] How would you run Document Understanding on-prem or in an air-gapped environment with limited GPU capacity?
    • What do you distil or quantise, and what accuracy loss is acceptable?
  14. [Likely] Design cross-document reasoning: match an invoice to a purchase order and a goods receipt and explain mismatches.
  15. [Likely] How would you detect drift in incoming document distributions (new vendors, new templates) and trigger action?

D. Communications Mining (email, chat and ticket understanding)

  Channels (email, chat, tickets, call transcripts)
     │
     ▼
  Thread reconstruction + PII masking
     │
     ▼
  Taxonomy (hierarchical labels, user-defined) ── Few-shot / active-learning training UI
     │
     ▼
  Inference: labels + extracted fields + sentiment/tone + quality-of-service signals
     │
     ├──> Analytics (volumes, drivers, SLA)
     └──> Automation trigger: robot / agent / human queue
  1. [Likely] Design a system that turns a bank's operations mailbox (200k emails per month) into structured requests that trigger automations.
    • How do you handle one email with several requests in it?
    • How do you handle threads where the intent changes mid-thread?
  2. [Likely] Customers define their own label taxonomies with very few examples. How do you design the model and training loop?
    • How do you pick examples for the user to label (uncertainty, diversity, coverage)?
    • How do you tell the user when a label is "good enough" to automate on?
  3. [Likely] [10] A UiPath ticket-classification sample has the LLM return a label with a self-reported confidence (for example 0.9) and sends it for human approval. Why is verbalised LLM confidence a weak basis for automation thresholds, and what would you put in its place per customer taxonomy?
    • How do you measure calibration per label when some labels have ten examples?
  4. [Likely] How would you decide between a fine-tuned encoder classifier, an embedding plus kNN approach, and an LLM prompt for intent classification at this scale?
    • What are the cost, latency and accuracy implications per million messages?
  5. [Likely] Month-end volume is 8x the daily average and some message types have a 15-minute SLA. Design capacity, prioritisation and graceful degradation for the inference and automation-trigger path.
  6. [Likely] Design field extraction from free-text messages (account numbers, amounts, dates) with validation against systems of record.
  7. [Likely] How would you discover new, emerging topics in communications that nobody has labelled yet?
    • How do you present clusters so a business user can act on them?
  8. [Likely] How do you evaluate a taxonomy-specific model when every customer's labels differ?
    • What platform-level metrics can you still track across customers?
  9. [Likely] How would you measure and improve service quality signals (tone, urgency, chaser emails) in a way that operations teams trust?
  10. [Likely] How would you handle multilingual mailboxes and code-switching?
  11. [Likely] [9] How does Communications Mining feed an agent rather than a fixed robot, and what changes in the design?

E. Process mining and task mining

  ERP / CRM event logs ──> Event log (case_id, activity, timestamp, attributes)
                                │
          ┌─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┐
          ▼                     ▼                         ▼
   Process discovery     Conformance checking      Predictive monitoring
   (graph of variants)   (deviations vs model)     (delay, outcome, next step)
          │                     │                         │
          └──────────> Automation opportunities ranked by value ──> Agent / robot design
                                                         ▲
   Task mining (desktop interaction logs, screenshots) ──┘
  1. [Likely] Design a system that takes ERP event logs and recommends which process steps to automate with robots, agents or neither.
    • How do you estimate the value and feasibility of each opportunity?
    • How do you avoid recommending automation of a step that only looks slow because of data quality?
  2. [Likely] How would you use LLMs to help build event logs from messy enterprise data (choosing case IDs, mapping tables to activities)?
    • How do you validate an LLM-proposed data model?
  3. [Likely] Design a predictive process monitoring system that forecasts SLA breaches for in-flight cases and triggers interventions.
    • How do you handle censoring and changing process versions?
    • How do you show the intervention actually caused the improvement?
  4. [Likely] How would you design task mining that infers repetitive desktop tasks from user interaction logs while respecting privacy?
    • How do you segment continuous interaction streams into tasks?
    • What do you blur, drop or keep on-device?
  5. [Likely] How would you close the loop so that process mining measures the impact of agents after deployment?
    • How do you represent agent steps (probabilistic, variable paths) in a process model?
    • How would you use conformance checking of agent trajectories against an approved process as an evaluation signal?
  6. [Likely] Design root-cause analysis over process variants that a business analyst can trust.

F. Autopilot (natural-language assistance across the platform)

  User prompt (in Studio / Apps / Assistant / Excel / email)
     │
     ▼
  Intent + context collection (open workflow, project, user permissions)
     │
     ▼
  Generation: workflow XAML / expressions / code / test cases / answers
     │
     ▼
  Validators: compile, static analysis, schema checks, sandbox run
     │                         │ fail
     ▼                         └──> repair loop (bounded) ──> fallback / ask user
  Suggestion shown to user ──> accept / edit / reject telemetry ──> eval + improvement
  1. [Likely] Design a feature that generates a UiPath automation workflow from a natural-language description.
    • What is the target representation the model generates, and why?
    • How do you validate that the generated workflow is correct before the user runs it?
    • How do you measure quality beyond "it compiles"?
  2. [Likely] How would you build an assistant that writes and fixes expressions and code inside Studio, given the project's context?
    • What context do you retrieve, and how do you fit it in the window?
  3. [Likely] Design automated test-case generation for automations from requirements documents.
    • How do you measure coverage and usefulness of generated tests?
  4. [Likely] How would you use accept/edit/reject telemetry to improve Autopilot without leaking customer data across tenants?
  5. [Likely] Autopilot answers questions about a customer's processes and data. How do you design retrieval and permission enforcement?
    • How do you stop the assistant from revealing data the user cannot otherwise access?
  6. [Likely] How would you prioritise model spend across Autopilot features with very different value per call?

G. Agent Builder, coded agents and agent evaluation

  Author defines: goal prompt, tools (robots, APIs, MCP, other agents), context (indexes), guardrails
     │
     ▼
  Design-time: eval sets, simulated tools, output + trajectory evaluators, LLM-as-judge
     │
     ▼
  Publish (versioned) ──> Runtime (orchestrated) ──> traces ──> monitoring + online eval
     ▲                                                              │
     └──────────── failure clusters / suggested fixes ◄─────────────┘
  1. [Likely] Design Agent Builder so that a citizen developer can build a reliable agent without being an ML expert.
    • Which decisions do you take away from the author, and which do you expose?
    • What defaults (models, guardrails, escalation) do you ship?
  2. [Likely] [17] [18] Design the evaluation system for agents: test sets, simulated tools, judges and release gates.
    • How do you generate realistic test cases when the author has none?
    • How do you validate an LLM-as-judge, audit it against human labels, and detect judge drift?
    • How do you evaluate the trajectory (tool choice, order, arguments), not just the final answer?
  3. [Likely] [17] [19] UiPath's SDK offers output evaluators (exact match, contains, JSON similarity, LLM judge) and trajectory evaluators (tool-call order, count, arguments, outputs, LLM-judge trajectory). For a refund agent, choose the evaluators per behaviour and design how their scores combine into a single release gate.
    • The tool-call-order evaluator gives partial credit through the longest common subsequence. When does that partial credit hide a dangerous ordering error (for example, refund before verification)?
    • Which checks must be hard gates rather than averaged scores?
  4. [Likely] [18] How would you simulate tools (robots, APIs) during agent testing so evaluation is safe and repeatable? UiPath's trajectory-simulation evaluator mocks tool responses from instructions while the agent does not know it is being simulated.
    • How do you check that the simulator itself behaved as instructed, so a failure is the agent's fault and not the mock's?
    • If the simulator and the agent use the same model family, what correlated errors do you worry about?
  5. [Likely] [17] Customers can register custom evaluators with their own domain logic. How do you let them do that safely, version the evaluators, and keep scores comparable across months?
  6. [Likely] [8] [17] LLM evaluations are noisy. Design the continuous-integration gate that runs an agent's eval set on every prompt, tool or model change.
    • How many runs per case, and what statistical test, before you call something a regression?
    • How do you keep the eval cost per change under a budget?
  7. [Likely] Design versioning and release management for agents: prompts, tools, models and context indexes all change independently.
    • What triggers a re-evaluation, and what blocks a release?
  8. [Likely] How would you automatically cluster agent failures from production traces and suggest fixes to the author?
  9. [Likely] [1] Design the context grounding (retrieval) component that agents use over enterprise content.
    • How do you handle permissions, freshness and document-level citations?
    • How do you evaluate retrieval separately from generation?
  10. [Likely] How would you optimise an agent's prompt and tool descriptions automatically (prompt optimisation) without overfitting to the test set?
  11. [Likely] How do you prevent an author from shipping an agent whose evaluation set is too small or too easy?

H. Maestro-style orchestration of long-running agentic processes

  Process model (BPMN-like) ──> Engine: state machine per case, timers, SLAs
        │                                  │
        │       ┌──────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┐
        ▼       ▼                          ▼                          ▼
   Agent tasks (probabilistic)   Robot tasks (deterministic)   Human tasks (queues)
        │                                  │                          │
        └───────── events, outputs, confidence, cost ─────────────────┘
                                   │
                                   ▼
             Case analytics + process mining on live instances ──> optimise routing / model
  1. [Likely] [8] Design an orchestration engine for long-running processes that mix agents, robots and humans, with SLAs measured in days.
    • How do you persist and resume state across failures and deployments?
    • How do you version the process model while thousands of cases are in flight?
  2. [Likely] [6] UiPath's docs warn that Maestro may retry a coded function after a transient failure, and advise checking for an existing result by a stable business key before writing. Extend that to agent tasks: on retry, the LLM may choose different tool arguments. How do you get exactly-once business effects?
    • Where do idempotency keys come from when the agent itself decides what to create?
  3. [Likely] [6] The same docs recommend returning business errors as typed output fields so the orchestrator can route on them. Design the error and uncertainty taxonomy for agent tasks so that Maestro can route "not sure", "policy blocked", "tool down" and "bad input" differently.
  4. [Likely] How would you design dynamic routing, where the orchestrator chooses agent, robot or human for each case at run time?
    • What signals feed the router, and how do you learn the routing policy safely (bandits, offline RL, rules)?
    • How do you evaluate a routing change before it goes live?
  5. [Likely] Where should "agentic" decisions live: inside the agent, inside the process model, or in the orchestrator? Defend your boundaries.
  6. [Likely] [5] [6] Design observability for an orchestrated agentic process: traces, cost per case, latency per step, quality per step. UiPath surfaces @traced spans in job traces and Maestro dashboards.
    • What is the trace schema that links process instance, agent run, LLM calls, robot jobs and human tasks?
    • How do you sample traces and strip PII without losing the ability to debug?
    • What would an on-call engineer look at first when case completion drops 10%?
  7. [Likely] How would you handle a mid-process LLM outage or a degraded model in an orchestrated process?
    • What is the fallback per task type?
  8. [Likely] How would you let an agent propose changes to the process model itself, and how would you govern that?
  9. [Likely] How would you design case-level explainability so an auditor can reconstruct why a case was approved?

I. Evaluation, reliability and quality across the platform

  1. [Likely] Design a single evaluation framework that covers DU extraction, Communications Mining classification, agent trajectories and computer-use tasks.
    • What is shared (datasets, runners, judges, dashboards) and what must stay specific?
  2. [Likely] How do you build and maintain golden datasets when customer data cannot leave the tenant?
    • How would you use synthetic data, and how would you check it is representative?
  3. [Likely] How would you run online evaluation and A/B tests for agents in enterprise processes where traffic is low and errors are expensive?
    • When do you use shadow mode, interleaving or staged rollout instead of A/B?
  4. [Likely] What is your reliability target for an agent step, and how do you compose step reliability into process-level reliability?
    • How does a 95%-reliable step compound across a 20-step process, and what do you do about it?
  5. [Likely] How would you detect silent quality regressions in production when there is no ground truth for most cases?
  6. [Likely] How would you measure and reduce hallucination in agents that write to systems of record?

J. Trust, safety, governance and deployment constraints

  1. [Likely] [15] Design an AI Trust Layer: a model gateway that enforces PII masking, policy, audit logging and usage quotas for every model call on the platform.
    • Where do you mask PII, and how do you unmask for downstream steps that need it?
    • What latency budget does the gateway get?
  2. [Likely] [15] [16] UiPath's guardrails apply at three scopes (tool, LLM call, whole agent), before and/or after execution, with actions such as log or block, and include deterministic rule checks alongside model-based detectors for PII, harmful content and prompt attacks. Design the guardrail architecture for a regulated agent.
    • Which risks belong at which scope, and why?
    • How do you evaluate the guardrails themselves (false-block rate, missed attacks), and who owns that eval?
  3. [Likely] [14] Customers can plug in their own guardrail vendor through a connector. How do you keep policy semantics consistent across vendors, and do you fail open or fail closed when the vendor is down?
  4. [Likely] [3] How would you support customers who require on-prem, sovereign cloud or "bring your own model" deployments?
    • How do you keep evaluation results valid across different underlying models?
  5. [Likely] How would you design permissions so an agent can never do more than the human or robot account it acts for?
  6. [Likely] How would you meet EU AI Act or sector rules (financial services, healthcare) for an agentic process?
    • What documentation, logging and human oversight would you build in by default?
  7. [Likely] Design the audit log for agent actions so it is complete, tamper-evident and still useful for debugging.
  8. [Likely] How would you handle data residency when an agent's model provider is in a different region than the customer data?

K. Cost, latency and model strategy

  1. [Likely] How would you decide between frontier API models, open-weight models you host, and small specialised models for each part of the platform?
    • What would make you train a UiPath-specific model rather than use a third-party one?
  2. [Likely] Design model routing (cascades) to cut cost by 60% while keeping quality on agentic tasks.
    • How do you decide when to escalate from a small model to a large one?
  3. [Likely] Design per-run budgets for agents (tokens, tool calls, wall-clock time) with hard caps. What should an agent do when it hits a cap mid-task: stop, summarise and escalate, or degrade to a cheaper model?
    • How do you set the caps per agent without starving the hard cases?
  4. [Likely] How would you build capacity planning and cost attribution per tenant, per agent and per process?
  5. [Likely] How would you cache LLM results safely in agentic workflows where inputs look similar but differ in important ways?
  6. [Likely] What is your approach to distilling frontier-model behaviour into a smaller model for DU or Communications Mining, and how do you check you lost nothing important?

L. Principal-level framing and roadmap probes

  1. [Likely] If you owned applied science for agentic automation, what would your 18-month research and product roadmap be?
    • Which bets are high-risk research, and which are engineering execution?
    • How would you staff and sequence them?
  2. [Likely] What are the three biggest open research problems for enterprise agents, and how would UiPath's data position help solve them?
  3. [Likely] Pick one UiPath AI product. What would you change in its architecture and why?
  4. [Likely] How do you decide when a capability should be a platform service versus a product-specific model?
  5. [Likely] [3] How do you keep the platform competitive as frontier models get better at computer use and tool use out of the box, and as customers bring agents built in any framework?
    • What is UiPath's durable advantage at each layer (orchestration, data, governance, models)?
  6. [Likely] How would you design a feedback flywheel from millions of robot and agent executions while respecting tenant data boundaries?

05. Coding and ML Coding for System Design Components: Building the Pieces of Document AI, Agent and Retrieval Systems

How to read this chapter. You asked for ML system design, so this chapter does not cover general LeetCode-style coding. It covers coding and ML coding questions where you implement one part of an ML system that you could be asked to design elsewhere in the loop: document and PDF processing, extraction and tagging, retrieval, agent tool loops, evaluation, and serving. Each section opens with a plain-text block diagram that shows which box of the system you are coding. Diagrams are plain text, so they render the same in Markdown and in a PDF export.

Tagging system. Every question carries one of two tags:

  • [Reported] [n]: a community-maintained list of LeetCode problems tagged to UiPath links this problem to UiPath interviews. These lists [3][4][5][6] are compiled from LeetCode's company tags. They do not name the role or level, and only one of them [4] has a date window (the last six months before its snapshot). Treat them as evidence that UiPath interviews use the problem, not that a Principal Applied Scientist was asked it. The sub-bullets under each [Reported] problem are [Likely] follow-up probes that connect the algorithm to a box in the system design diagram.
  • [Likely]: an inference from the role, the level and UiPath's AI products (Document Understanding, Communications Mining, agentic automation, Autopilot, the AI Trust Layer, computer vision for UI automation), or from UiPath's public open-source agent SDKs [7]-[13]. No candidate has reported it.
  • Indented sub-bullets are follow-up probes an interviewer may use to push a strong first answer toward the principal bar.

Research limits (please read). The shared web-search budget for this workflow was used up before this review, and DNS lookups failed for glassdoor.com, leetcode.com, uipath.com, docs.uipath.com, reddit.com, teamblind.com, ambitionbox.com, geeksforgeeks.org, interviewquery.com and arxiv.org. The only sources that could be fetched were raw files on GitHub and PyPI. So the [Reported] tags rest only on the UiPath-tagged LeetCode lists. No candidate write-up of a UiPath ML coding round could be read, and no ML-specific question below is tagged [Reported]. The format notes are inferred, not confirmed. Ask your recruiter whether the coding round is general algorithms, ML coding or both, and which language and environment to expect.

Format notes (inferred, not confirmed)

  • Duration: likely 45-60 minutes, in a shared editor or notebook, usually Python. There may be no execution environment, so you should be able to dry-run code by hand.
  • Interviewer: likely a senior or staff scientist or ML engineer, not a principal.
  • What is assessed at principal level: code that is correct and readable, without needing to be the fastest solution. The emphasis likely shifts toward whether you can turn a design box into a clean interface, pick sensible data structures, write tests and edge cases without being prompted, and explain how the component behaves at scale and how it fails. Expect the interviewer to connect the code to a system ("now this runs on 10 million pages a day").
  • Two common variants: (a) implement an ML primitive from scratch (attention, a metric, a decoding loop); (b) implement a pipeline component with a small amount of ML logic (chunker, matcher, tagger, evaluator).
  • A third variant with some evidence: a standard algorithm problem. The UiPath-tagged LeetCode lists [3][4][5][6] lean towards graph search (BFS, union-find), trees and dynamic programming at medium and hard difficulty. At principal level, expect the interviewer to ask how the algorithm maps to a real component (Section L).

How the coding round connects to the system design diagram

   ML SYSTEM DESIGN ROUND                         CODING / ML CODING ROUND
   (whiteboard, 60 min)                           (editor, 45-60 min)

   ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐       "Implement this box"
   │ Ingest & │─>│ Layout / │─>│ Extract/ │──┐        │
   │ OCR (A)  │  │ Tag (B)  │  │ Classify │  │        ├── A: PDF/OCR token handling, reading order
   └──────────┘  └──────────┘  │   (C)    │  │        ├── B: tagging, span/BIO decoding, tables
                               └──────────┘  │        ├── C: classifier, entity matching, validation
   ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐  │        ├── D: chunking, BM25, vector top-k, rerank
   │ Retrieve │<─│ Agent /  │<─│  Human   │<─┘        ├── E: agent loop, tool schema, retries
   │   (D)    │  │ LLM (E)  │  │ review(F)│           ├── F: confidence routing, active learning
   └──────────┘  └──────────┘  └──────────┘           ├── G: metrics and evaluation harness
         └────────── Eval (G) ── Serve (H) ──┘        └── H: batching, caching, rate limits

Questions

A. PDF and document ingestion: tokens, boxes and reading order

   PDF / scan ──> [ text layer? ] ──yes──> native tokens (text, bbox, font)
                       │ no
                       └──> OCR ──> words + bbox + confidence
                                     │
                                     v
                        [ YOUR CODE: normalise, sort, group into lines/blocks ]
                                     │
                                     v
                        reading-ordered tokens ──> tagger / extractor
  1. [Likely] Given a list of OCR words with bounding boxes (x0, y0, x1, y1) and text, write a function that groups them into lines and returns the lines in reading order.
    • How do you handle slight skew, where words on the same line have different y values?
    • What changes for a two-column page?
  2. [Likely] Write a function that detects whether a page has two or more text columns from word bounding boxes alone.
    • How would you test it without labelled data?
  3. [Likely] Implement intersection over union for two bounding boxes, then a function that merges overlapping boxes from two OCR engines into one set of words.
    • How do you decide which engine's text wins when the boxes overlap but the text differs?
  4. [Likely] Given native PDF text tokens and OCR tokens for the same page, align them so each OCR word maps to its native token or to nothing.
    • What is the complexity of your alignment, and how would you make it faster for dense pages?
  5. [Likely] Write a function that normalises bounding boxes from different page sizes and rotations (0, 90, 180, 270 degrees) into one coordinate system.
  6. [Likely] Implement a de-hyphenation and line-joining step that rebuilds paragraphs from line-broken OCR text.
    • Which cases would wrongly join two words, and how would you guard against them?
  7. [Likely] Write a streaming page iterator for a multi-thousand-page PDF batch that never holds more than N pages in memory.
    • How do you resume after a crash halfway through a file?
  8. [Likely] Given per-word OCR confidences, compute a page-level quality score and decide whether to re-OCR the page with a slower engine.
    • What threshold policy would you choose, and how would you tune it?

B. Tagging and structure: spans, key-value pairs and tables

   reading-ordered tokens ──> token classifier ──> per-token labels (B-/I-/O)
                                                        │
                                  [ YOUR CODE: decode labels -> spans/fields ]
                                                        │
                    ┌───────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────┐
                    v                                   v                   v
              key-value pairs                     table cells          document tags
  1. [Likely] Given tokens and BIO labels, write a function that decodes them into typed entity spans with start and end offsets.
    • What do you do with an I- label that follows O or a different entity type?
  2. [Likely] Implement Viterbi decoding for a linear-chain CRF given emission and transition score matrices.
    • How would you add a constraint that forbids illegal BIO transitions?
  3. [Likely] Write span-level precision, recall and F1 for entity extraction, with exact match and with partial (overlap) match.
    • Which one would you report to a product team, and why?
  4. [Likely] Given word boxes for a page, write a function that links each detected key ("Invoice No.", "Date") to its most likely value using spatial rules (right of, below).
    • How would you replace the rules with a learned scorer while keeping the same interface?
  5. [Likely] Given detected horizontal and vertical ruling lines and word boxes, reconstruct a table as a 2D grid of cell strings.
    • How do you handle merged cells and tables with no ruling lines?
  6. [Likely] Write a function that matches table header cells to a target schema (for example "Qty", "Quantity", "Units") using fuzzy matching.
    • Where would an embedding model beat string similarity here, and where would it be worse?
  7. [Likely] Implement a multi-label document tagger head on top of a pooled encoder output in PyTorch, including the loss.
    • How would you pick a decision threshold per tag?
  8. [Likely] Given a hierarchy of document tags (for example Finance > Invoice > Utility invoice), write a function that makes predictions consistent with the hierarchy.
  9. [Likely] Write a function that splits a multi-document PDF (several invoices scanned together) into separate documents from per-page classifier scores.
    • How would you formulate this as a sequence problem instead of page-by-page thresholds?

C. Extraction, normalisation and validation

  1. [Likely] Write parsers that normalise extracted dates, currency amounts and numbers across locales ("1.234,56", "1,234.56", "12/03/2025").
    • How do you resolve an ambiguous date like 03/04/2025 using other fields on the same document?
  2. [Likely] Implement a validator that checks invoice line items sum to the subtotal, and subtotal plus tax equals the total, within a tolerance, and reports which field is most likely wrong.
  3. [Likely] Given extracted vendor names and a master vendor list of 1 million entries, write a matcher that returns the top 5 candidates quickly.
    • Which index would you build: n-gram, phonetic, or embedding? Why?
  4. [Likely] Write a function that turns LLM output into a strict JSON object for a given schema, with repair for common errors (trailing commas, missing quotes, extra text).
    • When would you prefer constrained decoding over repairing output after generation?
  5. [Likely] Implement a field-level confidence aggregator that combines OCR confidence, model probability and validation-rule results into one score.
    • How would you check that the combined score is calibrated?
  6. [Likely] Write a regex-plus-model hybrid extractor for IBANs or tax IDs, including checksum validation.
  7. [Likely] Given extraction results from three models for the same document, write an ensembling function that votes per field.
    • How do you break ties, and how do you log disagreement for later review?

D. Retrieval and RAG components

   documents ──> [ YOUR CODE: chunk ] ──> embed ──> vector index
                                                        │
   query ──> embed ──> [ YOUR CODE: top-k + BM25 + fusion ] ──> [ rerank ] ──> LLM context
  1. [Likely] Implement a chunker that splits a document into overlapping chunks by token count while not breaking sentences, and that keeps page numbers and section titles as metadata.
    • How would you chunk a long table?
  2. [Likely] Implement BM25 scoring from scratch over a small corpus.
    • How do you update document frequencies when documents are added and deleted?
  3. [Likely] Implement exact top-k cosine similarity search with NumPy over a matrix of embeddings, and then explain how you would scale it.
    • Write the version that processes queries in batches without running out of memory.
  4. [Likely] Implement reciprocal rank fusion to merge BM25 and dense retrieval results.
  5. [Likely] Implement maximal marginal relevance (MMR) to pick diverse chunks for an LLM context window under a token budget.
  6. [Likely] Write recall@k, MRR and nDCG@k for a retrieval evaluation set.
    • How would you build the relevance labels for enterprise documents you cannot read?
  7. [Likely] Implement a simple inverted file (IVF) index: k-means over embeddings, assign vectors to lists, search the nearest n lists.
    • How does recall change with the number of lists probed?
  8. [Likely] Write a permission-aware retrieval filter so a user only gets chunks from documents they can access, applied before ranking.
    • Why is filtering after ranking a problem?
  9. [Likely] Implement a deduplication step for near-duplicate chunks using MinHash or SimHash.

E. LLM and agent components

   user goal ──> [ YOUR CODE: agent loop ] ──> LLM ──> tool call (JSON)
                       ^                                    │
                       │                  [ YOUR CODE: validate, execute, retry ]
                       └──────── observation <──────────────┘
                                         │
                            stop: answer / max steps / escalate to human
  1. [Likely] Write a minimal agent loop: call a model, parse a tool call, run the tool, append the observation, and stop on a final answer or a step limit.
    • Where do you enforce budgets for tokens, time and money?
  2. [Likely] Implement a tool registry that builds a JSON schema from Python function signatures and validates model arguments against it.
  3. [Likely] Write retry logic for tool calls with exponential backoff and jitter, and separate retryable from non-retryable errors.
    • How do you avoid running a non-idempotent action, such as sending a payment, twice?
  4. [Likely] Implement greedy, temperature, top-k and top-p (nucleus) sampling from a logits vector.
    • What breaks numerically with very low temperature, and how do you fix it?
  5. [Likely] Implement beam search for a toy sequence model.
  6. [Likely] Write a token-budget manager that fits a system prompt, retrieved chunks and conversation history into a context limit, dropping or summarising the least useful parts.
  7. [Likely] Implement a simple planner that turns a task into a DAG of steps and runs independent steps in parallel.
    • How do you handle one failing branch?
  8. [Likely] Write a PII redaction step (emails, phone numbers, card numbers, names) that runs before text is sent to an external LLM, and that can restore the values in the response.
    • How do you measure its recall?
  9. [Likely] Implement a prompt-injection heuristic filter for content read from documents and emails before it reaches an agent.
    • Why is a filter alone not enough, and what structural defence would you code instead?
  10. [Likely] Write a semantic cache for LLM calls keyed by embedding similarity, with a threshold and time-to-live.
    • What is the risk of serving a cached answer for a slightly different query?

F. Human-in-the-loop, confidence routing and active learning

   prediction + confidence ──> [ YOUR CODE: router ] ──> auto-accept (straight-through)
                                       │
                                       └──> human validation queue ──> corrected labels
                                                                              │
                                    [ YOUR CODE: sample for retraining ] <────┘
  1. [Likely] Write a router that sends each document to straight-through processing or human review based on field confidences and a target precision.
    • How would you choose the thresholds from a labelled validation set?
  2. [Likely] Implement temperature scaling for calibration and compute expected calibration error (ECE).
  3. [Likely] Implement uncertainty sampling (least confidence, margin, entropy) to choose which documents a human should label next.
    • How do you avoid choosing many near-identical documents?
  4. [Likely] Write a function that merges human corrections into a training set while tracking label provenance and annotator disagreement.
  5. [Likely] Compute Cohen's kappa for two annotators on a tagging task.
  6. [Likely] Given a stream of human corrections, write a drift detector that raises an alert when the correction rate for a field moves beyond normal variation.

G. Evaluation harnesses and metrics

  1. [Likely] Write a bootstrap confidence interval for the F1 difference between two extraction models on the same test set.
    • Why should you resample documents rather than fields?
  2. [Likely] Implement a field-level evaluation for document extraction that handles missing fields, extra fields and multi-valued fields such as line items.
  3. [Likely] Implement character error rate and word error rate for OCR output using edit distance.
  4. [Likely] Write an LLM-as-judge harness: run a judge prompt over model outputs, parse a score, and measure agreement with a small human-labelled set.
    • How would you detect position bias or length bias in the judge?
  5. [Likely] Implement a task-success evaluator for agent trajectories that checks final state rather than the exact sequence of actions.
  6. [Likely] Write a stratified sampler for an evaluation set across document types, languages and vendors with a fixed labelling budget.
  7. [Likely] Implement a paired sign test or McNemar's test to compare two classifiers on the same examples.

H. ML primitives from scratch

  1. [Likely] Implement scaled dot-product attention and multi-head attention in NumPy or PyTorch, including a causal mask.
    • What is the memory cost in sequence length, and how does it affect long documents?
    • Make the softmax numerically stable and fuse it with a cross-entropy loss; what overflows first in half precision?
  2. [Likely] Implement 2D sinusoidal or learned position embeddings for tokens with (x, y) layout coordinates, as in layout-aware document models.
    • How would you add bounding box information to a text transformer's input?
  3. [Likely] Implement a LoRA adapter layer that wraps a linear layer, with merge and unmerge methods.
  4. [Likely] Implement byte-pair encoding training on a small corpus and encoding of new text.
  5. [Likely] Implement a contrastive (InfoNCE) loss for training a retrieval embedding model with in-batch negatives.
    • What goes wrong with in-batch negatives when the batch has duplicates?
  6. [Likely] Implement non-maximum suppression for UI element or layout region detection.
  7. [Likely] Write a training loop in PyTorch with gradient accumulation, mixed precision and checkpointing.

I. Screen and UI understanding components

  1. [Likely] Given a list of detected UI elements (type, box, text), write a function that finds the element matching a natural-language target such as "the Submit button under Billing".
    • How would you make it robust to small layout changes between application versions?
  2. [Likely] Write a function that builds a containment tree from flat UI element boxes.
  3. [Likely] Implement a selector-repair function: when a stored selector no longer matches, find the most similar element on the new screen using attributes and position.
  4. [Likely] Given two screenshots' element lists, compute a diff of added, removed and moved elements.

J. Serving, scaling and production code

   requests ──> [ YOUR CODE: queue + dynamic batcher ] ──> model worker(s)
                         │                                       │
                   rate limiter                        [ YOUR CODE: cache ]
                         │                                       │
                   tenant isolation ───────── metrics / tracing ─┘
  1. [Likely] Implement a dynamic batcher that groups incoming inference requests up to a maximum batch size or maximum wait time.
    • How do you choose the wait time against a latency SLO?
  2. [Likely] Implement a token-bucket rate limiter per tenant for calls to an external LLM provider.
  3. [Likely] Implement an LRU cache with a size limit in bytes, then make it safe for concurrent use.
  4. [Likely] Write an async client that sends many document pages to a model endpoint with bounded concurrency and collects results in order.
  5. [Likely] Write a fallback router that sends a request to a smaller model first and escalates to a larger model when confidence is low.
    • How would you measure whether the router saves money without hurting quality?
  6. [Likely] Write a function that shards a large batch of documents across workers so that each worker gets a similar total page count.
  7. [Likely] Write structured logging for a model call that records inputs, outputs, latency and model version without logging customer PII.

K. Code review and principal-level judgement in the coding round

  1. [Likely] Here is a 60-line extraction pipeline written by a junior scientist. Review it: what is wrong, what is risky, and what would you change first?
    • Which of your comments block the merge and which are suggestions?
  2. [Likely] Turn the notebook prototype you just wrote into a module others can use: what interface, tests and configuration would you add?
  3. [Likely] Write unit tests for your reading-order or BIO-decoding function, including edge cases.
  4. [Likely] Your code works on the sample. The interviewer says it now runs on 10 million pages a day across 500 tenants. What do you change in the code, not only in the architecture?

L. UiPath-tagged algorithm problems, recast as system design components

   UiPath-tagged problem (graph / tree / DP)        Box in the ML system it maps to
   ─────────────────────────────────────────        ───────────────────────────────
   BFS / multi-source BFS  ───────────────────────> layout grids, UI state exploration (A, I)
   Union-find / connected components ─────────────> region merging, entity resolution (A, C)
   Sliding window / two pointers ─────────────────> OCR token streams, request batching (A, J)
   Tree DP / tree pruning ────────────────────────> UI element trees, agent plan trees (E, I)
   Interval DP / LIS ─────────────────────────────> document splitting, box nesting (B, I)
  1. [Reported] [3][5][6] Escape a Large Maze (LeetCode 1036): decide whether a target cell can be reached on a 10^6 x 10^6 grid with up to 200 blocked cells.
  • Why does a BFS bounded by the number of blocked cells give the right answer, and what is the bound?
  • An agent explores a large application state graph to reach a target screen. How would you bound its exploration in the same way, and when does the bound stop being valid?
  1. [Reported] [3][5] Sorting Three Groups (LeetCode 2826): find the minimum number of removals that make an array of values in {1, 2, 3} non-decreasing.
  • Recast it: per-page labels must follow the order cover page, body, appendix. Find the minimum number of relabels, and compare it with constrained Viterbi decoding.
  1. [Reported] [3][5][6] As Far from Land as Possible (LeetCode 1162): return the largest distance from any water cell to the nearest land cell.
  • Use a multi-source BFS distance transform on a rasterised page to find column gutters and white-space separators. How do you handle scan noise?
  1. [Reported] [3][5] Integer to Roman (LeetCode 12), then the reverse parser.
  • Write a robust parser for clause numbering in contracts ("IV.2(b)", "4.2.b", "Section iv") that returns a hierarchical section key. How do you test it on OCR errors such as "IV" read as "1V"?
  1. [Reported] [3][5] Making A Large Island (LeetCode 827): find the largest island possible after changing at most one 0 to 1.
  • Map it to connected-component labelling of text regions where one small gap splits a paragraph. How do you find the merge that helps most without merging two columns by mistake?
  1. [Reported] [3][5] Boats to Save People (LeetCode 881): use the fewest boats, each with a weight limit and at most two people.
  • Generalise it to packing documents of different token lengths into GPU batches under a memory limit. Why does the greedy two-pointer answer stop being optimal when a batch can hold more than two items?
  1. [Reported] [3][5] Satisfiability of Equality Equations (LeetCode 990): decide whether a set of "==" and "!=" constraints can all hold.
  • Turn it into vendor entity resolution with must-link and cannot-link constraints from human reviewers. What do you return when the constraints conflict, and to whom?
  1. [Reported] [3][5] Longest Repeating Character Replacement (LeetCode 424): find the longest substring that becomes one repeated character after at most k replacements.
  • On an OCR token stream with per-token confidence, find the longest span with at most k low-confidence tokens, as a candidate for one human review task.
  1. [Reported] [3][5] Collect Coins in a Tree (LeetCode 2603): find the minimum number of edge traversals that collect all coins within distance 2 and return to the start.
  • Prune an accessibility or DOM tree of thousands of UI elements to the smallest subtree that keeps every element an agent may need, before it goes into an LLM prompt.
  1. [Reported] [3][5] Burst Balloons (LeetCode 312): find the maximum coins from bursting balloons in the best order, using interval DP.
    • Use interval DP to split a scanned batch into documents so that the total segment score is highest. What is the complexity, and how would you reduce it for a 2,000-page batch?
  2. [Reported] [3][4][5] Min Stack (LeetCode 155): design a stack that returns the minimum element in O(1) time. It is one of only two problems in the six-month window of [4], and the only problem in the six-month list in the same repository as [5].
    • Extend it into an agent checkpoint stack that supports undo and returns the lowest-cost recoverable state in O(1) time. What must be persisted for a long-running agent that resumes after a human approval?
  3. [Reported] [3][5] Subsets II (LeetCode 90): return all subsets of a list that contains duplicates, without duplicate subsets.
    • Use it to enumerate tool-set or prompt-component ablations where some components are interchangeable. How do you cut the search when 2^n runs are too expensive to evaluate?
  4. [Reported] [3][5] Reorder List (LeetCode 143): reorder a linked list in place to L0, Ln, L1, Ln-1, and so on.
    • Write an in-place interleaving of results from two ranked retrievers that removes duplicates and keeps the reordering stable. When does round-robin interleaving beat score fusion?
  5. [Reported] [3][5] Russian Doll Envelopes (LeetCode 354): find the longest chain of envelopes that nest inside each other, in O(n log n) time.
    • Apply it to bounding boxes: find the deepest chain of nested layout regions (page, section, table, cell). Why does the trick of sorting by width ascending and height descending matter?
  6. [Reported] [3][4] Binary Tree Cameras (LeetCode 968): find the minimum number of cameras that monitor every node of a binary tree. This problem is in the six-month window of [4].
    • Recast it: choose the fewest human-approval checkpoints in an agent's plan tree so that every irreversible action is covered by an approval at that step or an adjacent one.

M. Agent runtime components on UiPath's public SDK contracts

   uipath.json / langgraph.json ──> [ YOUR CODE: entry points + JSON schema ]
                                              │
   input ──> Runtime.execute() / stream() ──> events (state, message) ──> result
                 │            │                                   │
          tools / MCP   context grounding (search)        structured error contract
                 │            │                                   │
          HITL: suspend ──> Action Center task ──> resume     traces (OpenTelemetry)
  1. [Likely] [9] Implement a class that satisfies a runtime protocol with get_schema, execute, stream and dispose, for an agent that extracts fields from an invoice PDF. stream should yield state events before the final result.
    • What must stay true between execute and the last event of stream, and how would you test it?
  2. [Likely] [9] Write a runtime factory that finds entry points from a config file, creates runtimes lazily, and reuses expensive resources (model clients, indexes) across executions without leaking state between tenants.
  3. [Likely] [9] Implement a structured error model with a code, a title, a detail and a category (user or system), and map tool, model and validation exceptions onto it.
    • Which errors should an orchestrator retry, and which should go to a human?
  4. [Likely] [8][11] Implement durable suspend and resume for a human-in-the-loop step: the agent creates a review task, saves its state, exits, and continues from the same point when the reviewer answers.
    • How do you make the resume idempotent if the callback arrives twice, or after the agent code has changed?
  5. [Likely] [7] Write a wrapper around a context-grounding search call (index name, query, number of results) that adds query rewriting, deduplication and a citation check before chunks reach the LLM.
    • How would you test the wrapper offline without access to the real index?
  6. [Likely] [10] Expose two document-processing functions (classify and extract) as tools on a small MCP server, with input and output schemas, timeouts and size limits for uploaded PDFs.
    • How does the agent side tell a tool failure apart from a tool result that says "no fields found"?
  7. [Likely] [9][12] Wrap any runtime so that each execution's spans and logs are isolated when several runtimes run in the same process. Record model latency, token counts and tool calls as OpenTelemetry-style spans. - What breaks if you use a global logger, and how do you show the bug in a test?
  8. [Likely] [7] Write a transaction-queue worker that takes documents from a queue, runs extraction, and marks each item as successful, failed with a retryable error, or failed with a business exception. - How do you avoid processing an item twice when a worker dies after the model call but before the status update?
  9. [Likely] [7][9] Write a function that reads Python entry-point functions with type hints or Pydantic models and creates the input and output JSON schemas that a packaging step would need. - Which Python types have no clean JSON Schema equivalent, and what do you do with them?
  10. [Likely] [11] Write an adapter layer so that one evaluation harness can run agents built with two different frameworks (for example LangGraph and LlamaIndex) behind one runtime interface. - What behaviour leaks through the adapter (streaming, interrupts, tool-call formats), and how do you keep the evaluation fair?

06. Principal-Level Leadership Through ML System Design: Technical Strategy, Design Reviews, Influence, Research-to-Product and Roadmaps

How to read this chapter. You asked for ML system design only, so this chapter does not cover general behavioral questions. It covers the leadership part of the Principal Applied Scientist bar as it shows up in ML system design: setting the technical strategy for an ML platform, running and defending design reviews, getting other teams to adopt a shared architecture, moving a research prototype into a production system, and planning a multi-quarter roadmap for systems such as Document Understanding (PDF and document tagging and extraction), agents and Communications Mining. Diagrams are plain-text block diagrams, so they render the same in Markdown and in a PDF export.

Tagging system. Every question carries one of two tags:

  • [Reported] [n]: a candidate report, question bank or official page links this question, or this round type, to UiPath. The number points to the References list.
  • [Likely]: an inference from the role, the level and UiPath's AI products (agentic automation, Document Understanding, Communications Mining, Autopilot, the AI Trust Layer, computer vision for UI automation). No candidate has reported it.
  • Indented sub-bullets are follow-up probes an interviewer may use to push a strong first answer toward the principal bar.

Research limits (please read). In the latest review run the shared web-search budget was used up, and direct fetches of uipath.com, docs.uipath.com, Glassdoor, Indeed, Levels.fyi, LeetCode Discuss and Blind were all blocked (DNS failure or proxy denial). Only GitHub raw files could be read. That means no candidate write-up of a UiPath leadership or design round could be checked, so no question below is tagged [Reported], and this chapter does not invent reports. Every question is [Likely]. Where a [Likely] question rests on a fact from a page that was read (for example UiPath's public agent SDKs, Action Center human-in-the-loop, context grounding, MCP hosting or runtime tracing), the question ends with (context: [n]). That marker points to the source of the product fact. It does not mean a candidate reported the question. The format notes describe the usual principal-scientist loop at enterprise AI companies of UiPath's size; they are not a confirmed UiPath process. Confirm round names and durations with your recruiter.

Format notes (inferred, not confirmed)

  • Where it happens. Leadership is unlikely to be tested only in a values round. At principal level it is probably tested inside the ML system design round ("now you have a team of six, what do you build first?"), in the research-presentation Q&A ("how did you get this adopted?"), and in a hiring-manager or cross-functional round with a director, head of AI or product leader. Each is typically 45-60 minutes. (Inferred; see chapter 01.)
  • Interviewers. Likely a principal scientist or architect for the design round, and a director or VP-level AI leader plus a product manager for the strategy and cross-functional rounds.
  • Bar assessed at principal level. (1) You set direction for a platform, not a single model. (2) You make irreversible architecture decisions with evidence and can say when you would reverse them. (3) Other teams adopt your design without reporting to you. (4) You turn research into shipped, measured product value. (5) You grow other scientists' design judgement. UiPath's published values are commonly cited as Humble, Bold, Immersed and Fast; the careers page [1] could not be reached in this run, so check the current wording yourself. Public interview-prep guides also note that GenAI and agent system design rounds in 2025-2026 tend to weigh evaluation, cost, latency, guardrails and human fallback above the architecture diagram itself [11].

Reference diagram: the leadership layers around an ML system design

 ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ STRATEGY       why this system, which bets, build/buy/partner, 2-3 yr view │
 ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
 │ ROADMAP        milestones, kill criteria, staffing, dependencies           │
 ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
 │ ARCHITECTURE   shared platform pieces vs product-specific pieces           │
 │   ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────────┐  ┌──────────────┐  ┌──────────────────┐   │
 │   │ Ingest / │->│ Models (LLM, │->│ Orchestration│->│ Human-in-the-loop│   │
 │   │ OCR/parse│  │ specialists) │  │ agents/robots│  │ validation/label │   │
 │   └──────────┘  └──────────────┘  └──────────────┘  └────────┬─────────┘   │
 │        ^                                                     │ feedback    │
 │        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘             │
 ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
 │ EVALUATION & GOVERNANCE   shared benchmarks, release gates, trust layer    │
 ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
 │ PEOPLE & ALIGNMENT  design reviews, RFCs, mentoring, partner teams, PM     │
 └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
