Questions

A. Technical strategy for ML systems

  1. [Likely] If you joined as the principal scientist for Document Understanding, what would your one-page technical strategy for the next two years say?
    • Which current architecture choices would you keep, and which would you challenge first?
    • What evidence would you gather in your first 90 days before committing to the strategy?
  2. [Likely] Specialist extraction models versus a general LLM or vision-language model for PDF and document extraction: how would you set the long-term direction, and how would you explain it to leadership?
    • Under what cost, accuracy or latency numbers would you reverse that decision?
    • How do you avoid stranding customers who have trained custom models on the old stack?
  3. [Likely] How would you decide which parts of UiPath's AI stack should be a shared platform and which should stay inside each product team?
    • Give an example of a component you would centralise (for example, evaluation or labeling) and one you would deliberately not centralise.
  4. [Likely] Build, buy or partner: how would you decide whether UiPath should train its own models, fine-tune open models or call frontier model APIs for a given capability?
    • How does customer choice of model provider change the architecture?
    • How do on-prem and air-gapped customers change the answer?
  5. [Likely] Describe a technical bet you made that most people disagreed with. How did you structure the system so the bet could be tested cheaply?
    • What was the kill criterion, and did you ever use it?
    • Give one bold architecture change that paid off and one that did not. What separated them?
  6. [Likely] How would you set the technical strategy for moving from deterministic RPA toward agentic automation without breaking customer trust in reliability?
    • Where must the system stay deterministic, and who decides?
  7. [Likely] How do you decide when an ML system needs a rewrite rather than incremental improvement?
    • How did you make that case to people who had built the old system?
  8. [Likely] How would you design a model strategy that survives the frontier-model landscape changing every few months?
    • What abstraction layer would you insist on, and what does it cost you?
  9. [Likely] What are the two or three ML system metrics you would put on an executive dashboard for an automation platform, and why those?
    • How do you connect straight-through processing rate or agent task success to customer ROI?
  10. [Likely] How would you identify the highest-leverage ML investment across Document Understanding, Communications Mining, agents and screen understanding when you can only staff two?
    • What data would you need, and what would you do if product leaders disagreed with your ranking?
  11. [Likely] UiPath's agent SDKs let teams and customers bring agents built in LangGraph, LlamaIndex, OpenAI Agents, Google ADK, Microsoft Agent Framework or PydanticAI onto one platform. Would you keep the platform framework-agnostic, or standardise on one framework for agents built in-house? (context: [5][6])
    • What must the shared runtime contract guarantee so that evaluation, tracing and human review work the same way for every framework?
  12. [Likely] How would you set the strategy for retrieval ("context grounding") across products: one shared, permission-aware index service, or retrieval owned by each product team? (context: [4])
    • Who owns index freshness, access control and retrieval quality metrics?
  13. [Likely] Would you expose document extraction and classification as MCP tools that third-party agents can call? Make the strategic case for and against. (context: [7])
    • How does that change your trust, metering and abuse-prevention design?
  14. [Likely] How would you set a cost-per-document or cost-per-agent-run target for the platform, and drive it down across teams you do not manage?
    • Which architecture levers (model routing, caching, distillation, batching) would you push first?
  15. [Likely] How would you set a strategy for evaluating agents that customers build in frameworks you do not control?

B. Leading design reviews and architecture decisions

  1. [Likely] Walk me through how you run a design review for a new ML system at principal level. What do you require in the design document?
    • What sections do people usually skip, and how do you make them write them?
  2. [Likely] A senior engineer proposes a document extraction architecture you think will not scale to multi-tenant use. How do you handle the review?
    • How do you separate "I would do it differently" from "this will fail"?
  3. [Likely] Tell me about an architecture decision record you wrote that was later proven wrong. What did you change in your process?
    • How did you tell the people who had followed your recommendation?
  4. [Likely] How do you decide which design decisions need broad review and which a team can make alone?
    • How do you classify reversible versus irreversible decisions in an ML system?
  5. [Likely] In a design review for an agent platform, which failure modes would you always ask about?
    • How do you make sure evaluation, cost and safety are reviewed, not only the model?
  6. [Likely] How do you review an ML system design when you are not the expert in its sub-domain (for example, OCR or speech)?
  7. [Likely] Describe a time you blocked a launch on design grounds. What was the evidence and how did you communicate it?
    • What would you have needed to see to approve it?
  8. [Likely] How do you keep a large ML architecture coherent when ten teams are each shipping changes to it?
    • What guardrails (interfaces, contracts, shared evaluation) would you put in place?
  9. [Likely] Two teams have built overlapping document classification services. How would you decide whether to merge them, and how would you lead that change?
    • How do you handle the team whose system is being retired?
  10. [Likely] How do you document an ML system so a new scientist can make safe changes in their first month?
  11. [Likely] You are reviewing the design of a long-running agent that pauses for human approval in Action Center and resumes hours later. What do you require on state, idempotency and resume semantics before you approve it? (context: [5][8])
    • What happens if the human edits the agent's proposed action instead of approving it?
  12. [Likely] A team proposes adding a new agent-framework integration to the shared runtime protocol. What conformance and evaluation checks must it pass before it ships? (context: [6][8])
  13. [Likely] You want every agent run to emit traces in one shared format (LLM calls, tool calls, human tasks). How would you get teams to agree on the span schema, and what would you do with teams that have already shipped their own? (context: [8][9])

C. Influencing without authority across teams

  1. [Likely] You want every product team to adopt one shared evaluation framework for agents and extraction models. None of them report to you. How do you get adoption?
    • What would you build first to make adoption cheaper than not adopting?
    • What do you do about a team that refuses?
  2. [Likely] How would you convince the platform engineering team to change their serving infrastructure to support a new model architecture your research needs?
    • How do you quantify the benefit in their terms rather than yours?
  3. [Likely] Tell me about a time you changed a product leader's mind about an ML system design using data.
    • What data did not persuade them, and why?
  4. [Likely] How do you align security, legal and product on PII handling in a document or communications ML pipeline?
    • Where would you put masking in the architecture, and how do you get sign-off?
  5. [Likely] A partner team owns the OCR component your extraction quality depends on, and their priorities differ from yours. How do you get the improvements you need?
  6. [Likely] How do you build credibility with engineering teams when you join as a principal scientist from outside?
  7. [Likely] How do you resolve a disagreement between research and engineering about whether a model is ready for production?
    • What shared release gate would you propose so the argument does not repeat?
  8. [Likely] Describe how you would get field and customer-success teams to feed real customer failures back into the ML system.
    • What tooling or process in the system design makes that feedback loop work?
  9. [Likely] How do you influence the direction of a system owned by a peer principal who disagrees with you?
  10. [Likely] How would you convince the team that owns the human-task (Action Center) experience to change its schema so validator corrections become usable training and evaluation labels? (context: [5])
    • What do you offer them in return?
  11. [Likely] A model provider deprecates the model version your extraction pipeline depends on, at short notice. How do you lead the response across your team, the provider and affected customers?
    • What would you change in the architecture so the next deprecation is routine?
  12. [Likely] A product team ships an agent feature that bypasses the shared guardrail and audit layer to meet a launch date. How do you respond, and how do you stop it happening again?
  13. [Likely] How would you use a customer advisory group to shape the design of a governance feature (model updates, data retention, audit) without letting the loudest customer set the architecture?

D. Research-to-product for ML systems

  1. [Likely] Walk me through a research result you took into production. What changed in the system design between the paper and the product?
    • Which offline metric gains disappeared in production, and why?
  2. [Likely] How would you take a new layout-aware or vision-language model for documents from a research prototype to a generally available Document Understanding feature?
    • What gates would you set between prototype, preview and GA?
    • How would you handle customers whose documents look nothing like the training data?
  3. [Likely] How do you decide which research directions are worth a production investment and which should stay exploratory?
  4. [Likely] How do you design the research environment so that experiments can be promoted to production without being rewritten?
    • What shared components (data loaders, eval harness, model registry) would you standardise?
  5. [Likely] How would you run a time-boxed research spike on computer-use agents, and how would you decide whether to productise it?
  6. [Likely] Tell me about a research project you stopped. How did you decide, and what did you salvage into the product?
  7. [Likely] How do you balance publishing research with protecting UiPath's product advantage?
  8. [Likely] How would you move a Communications Mining-style model from per-customer training toward a foundation model, and what organisational changes would that require?
  9. [Likely] How do you translate an academic benchmark gain into a business case a product leader will fund?
  10. [Likely] How would you take a research technique for agent reliability (for example self-verification or plan-then-execute) to production when it doubles token cost and latency?
    • What evidence of reliability gain would justify the cost, and for which customer processes?
  11. [Likely] How would you set up a loop where production failures from agents and extraction models directly set the research agenda?
    • Who triages failures, and how do they reach a researcher within a week?
  12. [Likely] How would you lead the move to synthetic documents for training and evaluating extraction models, and how would you get legal and customers comfortable with it?
  13. [Likely] How would you decide between per-tenant fine-tuning and a shared model with in-context examples for customer-specific extraction, and what does each mean for tenant isolation?

E. Roadmap and execution for ML platforms

  1. [Likely] Build a four-quarter roadmap for an enterprise agent evaluation platform. What ships in Q1?
    • Which dependencies on other teams are on the critical path?
    • What would you cut if your team were halved?
  2. [Likely] Build a roadmap for a document tagging and labeling system that reduces human validation effort by half.
    • How would you sequence active learning, confidence calibration and better base models?
    • Which milestone would tell you the plan is not working?
  3. [Likely] How do you plan ML work when model quality gains are uncertain and hard to estimate?
    • How do you communicate uncertainty in a roadmap to executives?
  4. [Likely] How do you balance roadmap items for new capabilities against reliability, technical debt and evaluation work?
  5. [Likely] A large customer asks for a custom model feature that conflicts with your platform roadmap. How do you decide?
  6. [Likely] How would you staff a new ML system: which roles, in which order, and which you would hire versus borrow?
  7. [Likely] How do you define success and kill criteria for each milestone of an ML platform roadmap?
  8. [Likely] Tell me about a roadmap you owned that slipped badly. What did you change in the system or the plan?
  9. [Likely] How do you plan the migration of customers from an older extraction model generation to a new one?
    • How do you prove to customers that the new model is not worse on their documents?
  10. [Likely] Build a roadmap that moves customers from robot-only workflows to processes that combine agents, robots and people. What has to be true in the platform before each step?
  11. [Likely] A new frontier model release makes two quarters of your team's specialist-model work look obsolete. How do you re-plan, and how do you explain it to your team and to leadership?
    • What part of that work would you keep, and why?
  12. [Likely] How would you set and track a reliability target (for example agent task success or straight-through processing rate) as a roadmap commitment rather than a slogan?

F. Cross-org alignment on platform architecture

  1. [Likely] How would you align Document Understanding, agents and Communications Mining teams on a shared model-serving and routing layer?
    • What would the interface contract look like, and who owns it?
  2. [Likely] How would you get agreement across product, research and the AI Trust Layer team on what every LLM call must log for audit?
  3. [Likely] How do you align cloud and on-prem product lines on one ML architecture when their constraints differ?
  4. [Likely] Describe how you would set up a cross-org architecture forum for ML systems. Who attends, and what decisions does it own?
    • How do you stop it becoming a bottleneck?
  5. [Likely] How do you handle a situation where two VPs want incompatible ML platform directions?
  6. [Likely] How would you align on a single definition of "agent task success" across teams that each measure it differently?
  7. [Likely] How do you manage shared labeling and human-validation infrastructure when several products compete for annotator capacity?
  8. [Likely] How would you align products and partners on one field schema and label taxonomy for document extraction?
    • Who owns changes to the schema, and how are breaking changes handled?
  9. [Likely] How would you get product and research to agree on one offline benchmark suite that gates every model release, when the most useful golden data is customer data under privacy limits?
  10. [Likely] A model-provider outage takes down agents, Document Understanding and Communications Mining at once. Who should own failover policy across products, and how would you get it agreed before the next outage?

G. Mentoring and raising design judgement

  1. [Likely] How do you teach a strong senior scientist to think about system design, not only model design?
    • What exercises or review habits do you use?
  2. [Likely] Tell me about someone you grew into a technical lead for an ML system. What did you delegate, and when?
  3. [Likely] How do you give feedback on a design document that is technically correct but misses the product problem?
  4. [Likely] How do you build an evaluation-first culture in a team that is used to shipping on demo quality?
  5. [Likely] How do you scale your design influence beyond the meetings you personally attend?
    • What templates, checklists or reference architectures have you created?
  6. [Likely] How do you mentor engineers who are new to ML on the failure modes of probabilistic systems in production?
  7. [Likely] What separates senior from principal design work in ML systems, and how have you coached someone across that gap?
  8. [Likely] How would you coach a strong researcher who resists production limits on latency, cost and on-prem deployment for a document model?
  9. [Likely] How would you set up design-review shadowing so junior scientists learn to review agent systems, not only build them?

H. Judgement, ownership and values in system decisions

  1. [Likely] Tell me about a time you shipped an ML system faster than the team thought possible. What did you deliberately leave out of the design?
    • What risks did that create, and how did you contain them?
  2. [Likely] Describe a time you took ownership of a production incident in an ML system you did not build.
    • What design changes did you drive afterwards?
  3. [Likely] When would you refuse to deploy an LLM-based automation for a customer process, even if the customer wants it?
    • How do you design the human-in-the-loop step so the customer stays accountable?
  4. [Likely] How do you handle a customer escalation where your model's extraction error caused a downstream financial mistake?
    • What changes in confidence thresholds, validation and audit would you lead?
  5. [Likely] How did you stay close to real customer usage ("immersed") when leading a platform used by thousands of customers?
  6. [Likely] How do you make a high-stakes design call when the data is incomplete and the deadline is fixed?
  7. [Likely] How do you weigh model cost against quality when the customer, finance and product each want different trade-offs?
  8. [Likely] What would you want your first design review at UiPath to accomplish, and how would you prepare for it?
  9. [Likely] An agent takes a wrong, irreversible action in a customer's ERP. Walk through how you lead the response and which design changes you drive afterwards.
    • How do you decide, as a company-wide policy, which agent actions need human approval?
  10. [Likely] When would you tell leadership that a much-hyped capability, such as fully autonomous computer-use agents, is not ready for enterprise customers, and what evidence would you bring?
  11. [Likely] How do you lead on fairness and quality gaps when your document or communications models are much weaker in some languages or regions?

I. Leadership probes attached to a live design prompt

These are likely to come mid-way through a design question, after you have drawn the architecture.

  1. [Likely] You have just designed this document extraction system. You have six people. Who works on which box first, and why?
  2. [Likely] Which box in your diagram is the riskiest, and how would you de-risk it in the first month?
  3. [Likely] Which parts of this design would you hand to a partner team, and how would you specify the interface?
  4. [Likely] If the product manager says the launch date moves up by two months, what do you cut from this design? - What do you refuse to cut?
  5. [Likely] How would you present this architecture to the CTO in five minutes?
  6. [Likely] What would make you scrap this design six months from now?
  7. [Likely] How would you measure whether this system is succeeding one year after launch, and who would you report it to?
  8. [Likely] Which decisions in this design would you want reviewed by security and legal, and when in the timeline?
  9. [Likely] Which component in your diagram would you build now as a shared platform piece, and which would you leave product-specific until a second team needs it?
  10. [Likely] If your LLM inference budget were cut by half, what changes in this diagram?
  11. [Likely] Which interface in your diagram needs an RFC first, and who must sign it?
  12. [Likely] How would you split this design between a small research team and a platform engineering team, and where are the hand-offs?

07. UiPath AI Context: DocPath and Document Understanding, the LLM Gateway and AI Trust Layer, Agentic Automation Strategy and the Peak Acquisition

How to read this chapter. This chapter asks how well you understand UiPath's own AI stack and how you would extend it. You asked for ML system design material, so each section opens with a plain-text block diagram of the UiPath system it covers, followed by numbered questions. Indented sub-bullets are follow-up probes. The chapter has questions only, with no answers. The diagrams are plain text so they look the same in Markdown and in a PDF export.

Tagging system. Every question carries one tag:

  • [Reported] [n]: a candidate report, question bank or official page links this question to UiPath. The number points to the References list.
  • [Likely]: an inference from the role (Principal Applied Scientist), the onsite loop format and UiPath's public product line. No candidate has reported it.
  • (context: [n]) at the end of a [Likely] question means the product fact in the question comes from a source that was read in the review pass. It does not mean a candidate reported the question.

Research limits (please read). In the review pass (October 2026) the shared web-search budget was already used up, and direct fetches of uipath.com, docs.uipath.com, Glassdoor, arXiv, Hugging Face and the OSWorld leaderboard site were blocked (DNS failure or proxy denial). Only raw GitHub files could be read. The review read 15 of them, mostly UiPath's own open-source SDK code and documentation (References 1-15). Those files confirm product facts: the LLM Gateway, AI Trust Layer model policy, guardrails, Document Understanding project types, Context Grounding, agent evaluation and human-in-the-loop. None of them is a candidate interview report. So no question below is tagged [Reported], and this chapter does not invent reports. Every question is [Likely].

Facts still unverified. The names DocPath and CommPath, the ScreenAgent name and its benchmark standing, and the details of the Peak acquisition (announced in 2025, by general knowledge) could not be checked against any page in this pass. Check them on uipath.com before the interview. One verified update: UiPath's Communications Mining CLI (the former Re:infer re tool) now describes itself as the CLI for UiPath IXP, and the SDK runs both document and IXP extraction through one Documents service (context: [5][12]).

Format notes for this round

Item Expected shape (inferred, not confirmed)
Where it appears It is rarely a separate round. It runs through the hiring-manager conversation, the system design round and the "why UiPath" part of the leadership or bar-raiser round.
Duration 10-25 minutes of a 45-60 minute round
Interviewer Hiring manager (an AI or science director), a principal scientist on DU, IXP, Agents or the platform, or a product leader
What is assessed at principal level Do you know what UiPath ships and why? Can you name the science gaps in it? Can you reason about the moat (specialised models, enterprise data, governance, the installed robot base) against frontier-model vendors? Can you propose a multi-quarter research agenda that fits the business?
Failure modes Generic "I'd fine-tune an LLM" answers; not knowing the difference between RPA robots and agents; ignoring on-prem, air-gapped and regulated customers; treating governance as an afterthought

1. Company AI strategy and "why UiPath"

  1. [Likely] UiPath has moved from RPA to "agentic automation". In your own words, what does that shift mean technically, and what new science problems does it create?
    • What can an agent do that a robot cannot, and where is a robot still the better choice?
    • How would you explain the shift to a customer who has 500 production robots?
  2. [Likely] What is UiPath's defensible advantage against frontier-model labs and hyperscalers that also ship agents?
    • Is the moat the models, the data, the orchestration layer, the installed base or governance? Rank them and defend the order.
    • What would make that moat disappear within two years?
  3. [Likely] What do you think is the single biggest open science problem in UiPath's product line today?
    • How would you staff and sequence a team to work on it?
  4. [Likely] UiPath sells to regulated enterprises (banking, insurance, healthcare, public sector). How does that change your research priorities compared with a consumer AI company?
    • Which methods would you rule out because of it?
  5. [Likely] Where should UiPath build its own models, and where should it rely on third-party LLMs?
    • What evidence would change your mind for a given capability?
    • What is your view on small specialised models against large general models for enterprise automation over the next three years?
  6. [Likely] How would you decide whether a research result is ready to go into a product that runs on customers' production processes?
  7. [Likely] What should a Principal Applied Scientist own at UiPath that a senior applied scientist does not?
    • Give an example of a cross-team technical decision you would expect to drive in your first year.
  8. [Likely] Model calls made through UiPath's agent SDKs consume the customer's "Agent Units" rather than the customer's own provider tokens. How does that pricing model change what the science team should optimise? (context: [3])
    • Who carries the cost of a model that uses three times more tokens for a two-point quality gain: UiPath or the customer?
    • How would you price a call to an in-house specialised model against a call to a frontier model?
  9. [Likely] UiPath now runs agents written in LangGraph, LlamaIndex, OpenAI Agents and other frameworks on one runtime, and also hosts customers' MCP servers. Is "be the neutral governed platform for everyone's agents" a stronger strategy than "build the best agents"? What does each strategy demand from applied science? (context: [13][14])

2. DocPath and Document Understanding (DU)

System diagram: the Document Understanding pipeline

  Document in (scan, PDF, email attachment, photo)
        │
        ▼
  ┌──────────────┐   ┌──────────────┐   ┌────────────────────┐   ┌──────────────────────┐
  │ Digitize     │──>│ Classify     │──>│ Extract             │──>│ Validate             │
  │ OCR / layout │   │ doc type,    │   │ project types:      │   │ confidence thresholds│
  │ reading order│   │ page split,  │   │ - pretrained        │   │ business rules       │
  │ tables, hand-│   │ results with │   │ - DU Modern         │   │ cross-field checks   │
  │ writing      │   │ confidences  │   │ - IXP (generative)  │   │                      │
  └──────────────┘   └──────────────┘   │ pinned to a project │   └─────────┬────────────┘
                                        │ version tag         │             │
                                        └────────────────────┘             │
                                                          low confidence   │  high confidence
                                                      ┌────────────────────┘──────────────┐
                                                      ▼                                   ▼
                                        ┌──────────────────────┐            ┌────────────────────┐
                                        │ Classification and   │            │ Export to robot /  │
                                        │ extraction validation│            │ agent / ERP        │
                                        │ tasks (Action Center)│            └────────────────────┘
                                        └──────────┬───────────┘
                                                   │ corrected labels
                                                   ▼
                                        ┌──────────────────────┐
                                        │ Active learning /     │
                                        │ retraining, new tag,  │
                                        │ eval sets, promotion  │
                                        └──────────────────────┘
  1. [Likely] DocPath is positioned as a specialised generative model for documents. Why would a company build a document-specific LLM instead of prompting a general frontier model?
  • On which document types would you expect a general model to win, and on which DocPath?
  • What benchmark would convince a sceptical product leader?
  1. [Likely] Design the training data strategy for a document foundation model when most customer documents are confidential and cannot leave the tenant.
  • What can you do with synthetic documents, and where does synthetic data fail?
  • How would you use human validation corrections without breaking data-use commitments?
  1. [Likely] How do you produce calibrated per-field confidence scores from a generative extractor so that the validation-threshold logic in the diagram still works?
  • How would you measure calibration per field and per document type?
  • What happens to straight-through-processing rate if the model is overconfident by 5 points?
  1. [Likely] Generative extraction can hallucinate a plausible invoice number that is not in the document. How do you detect and prevent that?
  • How would you ground each extracted value to a bounding box or token span?
  • What is your metric for "unsupported" values?
  1. [Likely] Compare three extraction approaches for a new document type with 20 labelled samples: a fine-tuned layout model, a prompted generative model and a hybrid. How do you choose?
  • How does the answer change at 2,000 samples?
  1. [Likely] UiPath's SDK exposes three extraction project types side by side: pretrained models, DU Modern projects and IXP projects. If you owned the roadmap, would you converge them into one model family or keep them separate? (context: [5])
  • What does each type buy the customer in cost, latency, controllability and labelling effort?
  • How would you migrate a customer's trained DU Modern project to IXP without a drop in accuracy?
  1. [Likely] How would you handle multi-page bundles (a loan file with 40 mixed documents) for classification and page splitting?
  • Where do long-context models help here, and where do they hurt?
  1. [Likely] The SDK's default extraction path takes several classification results and keeps the one with the highest confidence. When is "take the argmax class" the wrong policy for a document pipeline? (context: [5])
  • How would you handle near-ties, out-of-taxonomy documents and bundles that contain several types?
  • Should a low classification margin open a classification-validation task even when extraction confidence is high?
  1. [Likely] Tables, checkboxes, signatures, stamps and handwriting cause most DU errors. How would you prioritise model work across them?
  • What error analysis would you run first?
  1. [Likely] Design the evaluation framework for DU across hundreds of customer document types.
  • Field-level F1, document-level accuracy or straight-through rate: which is the north-star metric and why?
  • How do you build a held-out set that reflects the customer distribution without seeing customer data?
  1. [Likely] A large customer reports that extraction accuracy fell after a model upgrade, while your global benchmark improved. How do you investigate, and how do you prevent this?
  2. [Likely] DU and IXP extraction calls name a published project version by tag (or version number). Design the release process for a customer's project: how a retrained model earns a new tag, how it is shadow-tested against the current tag, and when traffic moves. (context: [5])
  • What offline and online evidence do you require before a customer's production tag moves?
  • How do you roll back if straight-through rate drops after promotion?
  1. [Likely] Human review in the SDK is split into classification validation and extraction validation tasks in Action Center. Treat human validation capacity as a fixed budget: how would you allocate it across documents and fields to maximise end-to-end accuracy? (context: [5][10])
  • How does the policy change when the validation queue backs up and SLAs are at risk?
  1. [Likely] IXP extraction in the SDK is asynchronous: start the job, then poll for the result. How would you schedule GPU inference for a mix of one-page receipts and 300-page contracts under per-tenant SLAs? (context: [5])
  • Where would you batch, where would you split documents, and how would you decide?
  1. [Likely] How should OCR and the generative extractor interact? Would you move to an OCR-free, pixel-input model?
  • What does that do to latency, cost and multilingual coverage?
  1. [Likely] Design an active-learning loop that uses human validation in Action Center to improve a tenant's extractor without catastrophic forgetting or label noise.
  • How do you handle validators who disagree or who make systematic mistakes?
  1. [Likely] How would you serve DocPath for on-premises and air-gapped customers with limited GPUs?
  • Which compression methods would you try (distillation, quantisation, smaller specialised heads), and how do you show that quality held?
  1. [Likely] How do you extend document models to new languages and scripts (Arabic, Japanese, Cyrillic) with little labelled data?
  2. [Likely] What role should DU play inside an agent? Should extraction be a tool the agent calls, or should the agent read documents directly?
  • How do confidence and validation signals pass through to the agent's decisions?

3. CommPath, Communications Mining and IXP

System diagram: communications understanding

  Email / ticket / chat streams
        │
        ▼
  ┌──────────────┐   ┌────────────────────┐   ┌───────────────────────┐   ┌────────────────────┐
  │ Ingest,      │──>│ CommPath / intent  │──>│ Entity + field        │──>│ Route: robot, agent│
  │ thread, de-  │   │ taxonomy model     │   │ extraction, sentiment,│   │ human queue, or    │
  │ dup, redact  │   │ (multi-label,      │   │ urgency, quality-of-  │   │ auto-reply draft   │
  └──────────────┘   │  hierarchical)     │   │ service signals       │   └────────────────────┘
                     └─────────┬──────────┘   └───────────────────────┘
                               │ taxonomy drift / new intents
                               ▼
                     ┌────────────────────┐
                     │ Few-shot labelling │  <── analyst trains taxonomy in UI
                     │ + discovery        │      (sources, datasets managed via
                     └────────────────────┘       the IXP `re` CLI)
  1. [Likely] Communications Mining lets business users build their own intent taxonomy with few labels. How would you design the model so a non-expert gets a usable classifier after about 50 labels?
  • How do you choose which messages to show the user next?
  1. [Likely] How would you discover new or emerging intents in a stream of customer emails before anyone has labelled them?
  • How do you avoid flooding analysts with junk clusters?
  1. [Likely] Why build a communications-specific model (CommPath) rather than prompt a general LLM per message?
  • Answer in terms of cost per million messages, latency and stability of the taxonomy.
  1. [Likely] UiPath's Communications Mining tooling now presents itself as part of UiPath IXP, alongside document extraction. What is the science case for one extraction platform over emails and documents, and what breaks when you merge them? (context: [5][12])
  • Can one label schema and one model family cover an email thread with three PDF attachments?
  • How would you migrate existing Communications Mining datasets and taxonomies without retraining every customer's model from scratch?
  1. [Likely] How do you evaluate a hierarchical multi-label intent model when the taxonomy changes every month?
  2. [Likely] Design the handoff from a classified email to an agent that drafts a reply and a robot that updates the CRM. Where are the human checkpoints?
  • What failure would worry you most in that chain?

4. LLM Gateway and AI Trust Layer

System diagram: governed LLM access

  Caller (Agent, coded agent, DU/IXP, Autopilot, customer workflow)
        │  request + tenant + requesting product/feature headers
        ▼
  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ AI TRUST LAYER / LLM GATEWAY                                                │
  │                                                                             │
  │  ┌───────────┐  ┌──────────────┐  ┌──────────────┐  ┌────────────────────┐  │
  │  │ AuthN/Z,  │─>│ Policy check │─>│ Guardrails   │─>│ Router: passthrough│  │
  │  │ tenant    │  │ (allowed     │  │ PRE: PII,    │  │ (native provider   │  │
  │  │ quotas,   │  │ models, data │  │ prompt attack│  │ API) or normalized │  │
  │  │ Agent     │  │ residency)   │  │ BYOG vendors │  │ API; schema cleanup│  │
  │  │ Units     │  └──────────────┘  └──────────────┘  └─────────┬──────────┘  │
  │  └───────────┘                                                │             │
  │  ┌─────────────────────────────┐                     ┌────────▼─────────┐   │
  │  │ Audit log · usage + cost by │<────────────────────│ Guardrails POST: │   │
  │  │ product/feature · traces    │                     │ PII, harmful, IP,│   │
  │  └─────────────────────────────┘                     │ LLM-as-judge     │   │
  │                                                      └──────────────────┘   │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
        │
        ▼
  Model providers: UiPath models (DocPath, CommPath), OpenAI, AWS Bedrock (Anthropic), Google Vertex AI
  1. [Likely] What should an enterprise LLM gateway do that a direct API call to a model provider does not?
  • Which of those functions need applied-science work rather than plain engineering?
  1. [Likely] UiPath's gateway offers two interfaces: passthrough classes that expose each provider's native API, and a normalized interface that is uniform across providers but loses provider-specific features. The SDK docs recommend passthrough. Which would you make the default for UiPath's own agents, and why? (context: [2][3])
  • What does each choice do to portability, to evaluation and to the cost of switching providers later?
  1. [Likely] The models a tenant can call depend on that tenant's AI Trust Layer policy. An agent built and evaluated on one model is deployed to a tenant whose policy blocks that model. Design what should happen. (context: [3])
  • How do you guarantee quality on a substitute model you never tested the agent with?
  • Should the platform refuse to deploy, fall back automatically, or re-run the agent's evaluation set first?
  1. [Likely] Design PII and PHI detection and masking for prompts going to third-party LLMs.
  • How do you mask without destroying the information the LLM needs (names in a contract, account numbers in an invoice dispute)?
  • What is your recall target, and how do you measure it across languages?
  • How do you re-identify the masked values safely in the response?
  1. [Likely] UiPath's PII guardrail takes a per-entity confidence threshold (for example email at 0.5). How would you pick thresholds per entity type, language and customer, and what evidence would you show a compliance officer for each choice? (context: [4])
  • Is one threshold per entity calibrated across languages, or do you need per-language calibration?
  1. [Likely] Guardrails can run at three scopes (tool, LLM call, whole agent) and at PRE, POST or both stages. Where would you place each validator for a claims-processing agent, and what latency and cost budget would you set for the guardrail stack? (context: [4][16])
  • Which checks are worth a model call on every step, and which only at agent input and output?
  1. [Likely] The user-prompt-attack validator runs only before a call, not after. Most real prompt injection reaches an agent indirectly, through documents, emails, web pages or tool outputs. How would you design detection for that indirect path? (context: [4])
  • Where does that check belong: in the gateway, in the agent runtime, or in both?
  • How do you measure false positives that break legitimate automations?
  1. [Likely] UiPath offers an LLM-as-judge guardrail at runtime, alongside fixed classifiers. When is an LLM judge a sound runtime guardrail, and when is it a liability? (context: [4])
  • What happens when the judge and the agent run on the same model family?
  • How do you bound the extra latency and cost per run?
  1. [Likely] "Bring Your Own Guardrail" lets an admin plug a third-party guardrail vendor into the Trust Layer. How would you qualify a customer's guardrail vendor, and what should happen when it disagrees with UiPath's built-in validator? (context: [4])
  2. [Likely] UiPath's guardrails include an "intellectual property" validator for outputs. What could that mean technically (code licence detection, copyrighted text, trade secrets), and how would you build and evaluate it? (context: [4])
  3. [Likely] A guardrail can log, block or modify (redact) the data it inspects. For a multi-step agent, when should a violation block the run, and when should it redact and continue? (context: [4])
  • How does a silent redaction change the agent's later reasoning, and how would you detect harm from it?
  1. [Likely] Design a model router that picks between a small specialised model and a frontier model per request.
  • What features does the router use, and what does it optimise (cost, latency, quality, policy)?
  • How do you train and evaluate the router without ground-truth labels for every request?
  1. [Likely] Customers can bring their own model or API keys. How do you keep agent and DU quality guarantees when the underlying model is not yours?
  • What compatibility test suite would you require before a customer model is allowed?
  1. [Likely] UiPath's gateway rewrites JSON schemas for structured output (it strips titles and forces additionalProperties: false), and it handles provider quirks such as reasoning models that reject a temperature setting. How would you guarantee schema-valid structured output across OpenAI, Anthropic and Gemini models that differ in strict-mode support? (context: [2])
  • How would you maintain and test a capability matrix of models against features (tools, schemas, reasoning effort, vision)?
  1. [Likely] Every gateway call carries "requesting product" and "requesting feature" headers. Design cost and quality attribution across UiPath products from that telemetry. (context: [2])
  • How would you use it to find which product feature gives the worst quality per dollar?
  1. [Likely] The SDK's model list still contains old model versions next to new ones. Design a deprecation process for a model that thousands of customer agents are pinned to. (context: [2])
  • What regression evidence do you need before forcing a migration, and who signs off?
  1. [Likely] Design the audit and observability layer so a compliance officer can reconstruct why an agent took an action six months later.
  • What do you log, and what must you not log?
  1. [Likely] How would you design semantic caching in the gateway, and when is it unsafe?
  2. [Likely] A new frontier model is released. Walk through how you would qualify it before turning it on for all tenants.
  • What regression suites, canary strategy and rollback triggers would you use?
  1. [Likely] How would you enforce data residency (EU-only processing, for example) while still giving customers access to the best models?
  2. [Likely] What guardrail models would you build in-house (toxicity, groundedness, policy adherence, structured-output validity), and which would you buy?
  3. [Likely] How should cost and token budgets be enforced per tenant, per agent and per run, and how do you stop a looping agent from burning budget?

5. Agentic automation: Agent Builder, Maestro, Context Grounding and computer use

        │

  1. [Likely] UiPath frames its approach as agents, robots and humans working together. Design how Maestro should decide which of the three handles each step.
  • Is that decision made at design time, at runtime, or both?
  • How do you learn better routing from historical runs?
  1. [Likely] Design the evaluation system for agents built by customers in Agent Builder, who have few test cases and no ML background.
  • How would you generate evaluation cases automatically from process documentation or past runs?
  • When is LLM-as-judge acceptable, and how do you calibrate it against humans?
  1. [Likely] UiPath's agent evaluation framework splits evaluators into output-based ones (exact match, contains, JSON similarity, LLM judge) and trajectory-based ones (tool-call order, count, arguments and outputs, LLM judge of the trajectory). When does a trajectory evaluator punish a correct agent, and how would you fix that? (context: [8])
  • How would you score an agent that reaches the right answer by a different but valid tool order?
  1. [Likely] The LLM-judge output evaluator returns a score from 0 to 1 with a written justification. How would you turn that score into a pass/fail release gate, and how do you stop the gate drifting when the judge model is upgraded in the gateway? (context: [9])
  2. [Likely] How would you make a non-deterministic agent safe enough to run inside a process that auditors expect to be repeatable?
  • What would "deterministic replay" mean for an LLM agent?
  1. [Likely] UiPath agents suspend on interrupt(...) while a person handles an Action Center task, then resume when it is done. Design the checkpoint for a run that waits three days for a human. (context: [10])
  • What state must be saved, and what must be re-read on resume?
  • What should happen if the prompt, model or policy changed while the run was suspended?
  1. [Likely] UiPath's computer-use work has been reported on public desktop-agent benchmarks such as OSWorld (check the current standing before the interview). What do such benchmarks miss about enterprise desktop automation?
  • How would you build an internal benchmark for legacy apps (Citrix, SAP GUI, mainframe terminals)?
  1. [Likely] OSWorld now runs a "Verified" leaderboard where the maintainers run the agent code themselves instead of accepting self-reported scores. How should UiPath report computer-use results so that enterprise buyers and researchers trust them? (context: [15])
  • What would you publish beyond a single success rate (cost per task, steps, failure taxonomy, variance across runs)?
  1. [Likely] Design a computer-use agent that uses existing RPA selectors and recorded workflows as priors instead of acting from raw pixels.
  • How do you recover when the UI changes and a selector breaks?
  • How do you decide between a grounding model, the accessibility tree and OCR for a given screen?
  1. [Likely] How would you turn the large base of existing RPA workflows into training data or tools for agents?
  • What are the licensing and tenant-isolation constraints?
  1. [Likely] Design "self-healing" automation: an agent that fixes broken robot workflows at runtime. How do you stop it from silently doing the wrong thing?
  2. [Likely] How should Context Grounding (enterprise retrieval) be built so agents get the right policy document and not a stale or out-of-scope one?
  • How do you evaluate retrieval quality per tenant without labelled queries?
  1. [Likely] Context Grounding indexes live in Orchestrator folders, and a search can fall back to tenant-wide system indexes when the named index is not found. What permission-leak and wrong-context risks does that create, and how would you design retrieval to enforce document-level permissions? (context: [6][7])
  2. [Likely] Context Grounding's Deep RAG runs a research-style synthesis across many documents in an index, with a choice of citation mode. How would you evaluate the faithfulness and citation accuracy of a multi-document synthesis? (context: [6])
  • When should an agent be forced to cite, and what should happen when a claim has no supporting passage?
  1. [Likely] Batch Transform applies one LLM prompt to every row of a CSV in an index, adds new output columns, and can optionally ground on web search. Design quality control for row-level LLM transformations over a million rows. (context: [6])
  • How do you sample rows for review, and how do you detect systematic errors?
  • What new risks does web-search grounding bring into an enterprise data pipeline?
  1. [Likely] Unified search supports a semantic mode and a tabular mode, and can return partial results while ingestion is still running. How would you route a natural-language query between unstructured and tabular retrieval, and when is it unsafe for an agent to act on partial results? (context: [6])
  2. [Likely] Context Grounding can build an ephemeral index for one run's attachments. When is per-run indexing better than putting the whole document set into a long-context model, and how would you decide by cost and quality? (context: [6])
  3. [Likely] Autopilot helps developers and business users build automations from natural language. How would you measure whether Autopilot-generated workflows are correct?
  • Would you use execution-based evaluation, and how would you sandbox it?
  1. [Likely] How would you use Process Mining and Task Mining data to find which processes are worth turning into agents?
  • How do you estimate the ROI of agentifying a process before building it?
  1. [Likely] What are the main failure modes of multi-step enterprise agents you have seen or expect, and how would you instrument for each?
  • Which ones can a better model fix, and which need system design?
  1. [Likely] How would you design agent memory across a long-running case that lasts weeks (a claims process, for example)?
  2. [Likely] Should UiPath train its own agent or action models, or orchestrate third-party ones? Lay out a decision framework.
  3. [Likely] How would you support open agent protocols (such as MCP and agent-to-agent interop) while keeping governance through the Trust Layer? (context: [13])
  • What new attack surface does a customer-hosted MCP server open?

Where would you invest science effort first?

08. End-to-End ML System Design: A PDF Tagging System (Structure, Accessibility and Document Understanding)

How to read this chapter. The rest of the pack touches PDF tagging in scattered pieces: chapter 04 section C (Document Understanding), chapter 05 sections A and B (ML coding on layout and tables), and the leadership diagram in chapter 06. This chapter puts the whole system in one place. It opens with one end-to-end diagram. Each section then has a smaller diagram for its stage, numbered questions and follow-up probes. It has questions only, with no answers. The diagrams are plain text so they render the same in Markdown and in a PDF export.

Tagging system. Every question carries one of two tags:

  • [Reported] [n]: a candidate report links this question to UiPath. The number points to the References list.
  • [Likely]: an inference from the role (Principal Applied Scientist), the level and UiPath's document products: Document Understanding (DU Modern), IXP, validation in Action Center, and published project versions and tags. No candidate has reported it. A [Likely] [n] tag means source [n] confirms the premise of the question, such as a product behaviour, dataset or metric definition. It does not mean a candidate was asked it.
  • Indented sub-bullets are follow-up probes an interviewer may use to push a strong first answer toward the principal bar.

"Tag" has two meanings here. (1) A PDF structure tag: an element of the tagged-PDF structure tree, such as H1, P, L/LI, Table/TR/TH/TD, Figure with alt text, or Span. This is what PDF/UA accessibility needs. (2) A semantic tag or label: a document class, a key-value field or an entity, as in Document Understanding and IXP. UiPath also uses "tag" for a published project version [1]. A principal-level answer names which meaning it is using, and the chapter tests both.

Research limits (please read). Two passes (October 2026) checked this chapter. In both, the workflow's shared web-search budget was already used up, so no search could run. The fetch proxy could not resolve glassdoor.com, teamblind.com, uipath.com, docs.uipath.com, arxiv.org, w3.org or pdfa.org. Only GitHub-hosted sources were reachable. The second pass re-fetched all 11 original sources and confirmed each claim the questions rely on. It also fetched 15 more GitHub sources [12]-[26] on benchmarks, OCR, table metrics, label quality and the PDF/UA rule set. These sources confirm product behaviour, datasets and metric definitions, not interview content. So no question below is tagged [Reported], and the chapter does not invent reports. The UiPath documentation portal could not be reached, so it was removed from the references. Check product names and current PDF/UA details (ISO 14289-1 and -2, the Matterhorn Protocol, the PDF 2.0 structure namespace) against primary documents before the interview.

Format notes for this round

Item Expected shape (inferred, not confirmed)
Round ML / applied science system design. For a document-AI team it may be a deep dive on one document pipeline, or a design prompt followed by a probe on your own past document work
Duration About 60 min: 5 min scoping, 30-35 min design, 15 min deep dives (evaluation, labelling, scale), 5 min your questions
Interviewer Principal or staff scientist, or an architect from the document / IXP side; possibly a product manager for the human-review and customer angle
Likely prompt "Design a system that turns any incoming PDF (native or scanned) into a correctly tagged, accessible PDF and structured data, for enterprise customers at scale"
Principal-level bar You define the tag taxonomy and the success metrics before you pick models. You treat evaluation, labelling and human review as first-class systems, not afterthoughts. You separate the deterministic work (PDF parsing, schema validation, PDF/UA checks) from the probabilistic work (layout, reading order, semantics). You reason about cost per page, latency tiers, on-prem and regulated deployment, and versioning. You name the open research problems and a multi-quarter plan.
Common failure modes Treating it as "just OCR plus an LLM"; no reading-order story; ignoring nested tables and multi-page objects; no plan for the confidence threshold and the review queue; metrics that do not match what the customer (screen-reader user, auditor or downstream robot) experiences
